“Huh... haha. Well then.”

The listener shook his head repeatedly. The more he heard, the stranger the matters became.

“They eat so well, but why do the people you see passing by look like that? I thought the Beggars’ Union had joined the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“Heh heh heh. That’s right, that’s right. I heard they train more intensely than they eat.”

The speaker shook his head.

“I thought those great prestigious Justice faction members were like lofty cranes above the sky, too noble to even look up to, but after seeing their training, I’m no longer envious.”

“Envious? I’d run away even if they offered me free admission. What kind of thing is that for a person to do?”

“Exactly.”

No matter how much the major martial sects try to get closer to the common people, that distance is not easily closed. Because people cannot easily shake off the aversion to those who carry swords even in peacetime.

Even if they intellectually understand that these people are good, the fear that they could be cut down with a single sword at any time is something anyone would inevitably have.

But now, the Heavenly Friends Alliance was closing the gap with the common people in a way even they did not expect.

“I heard they also hired a large number of cooks this time?”

“Don’t even mention it. They hired thirty new cooks this time. Besides that, they also hired people to manage the manor and care for the beasts.”

“Haha. Well then.”

“It’s fortunate. How fortunate. So many were half-starving because those bandits ran wild and couldn’t even go hunting. Now those people all go to that manor to work and get paid. Thanks to that, incidents in the river mouth have some breathing room.”

“But... is the Heavenly Friends Alliance really that rich? I heard that food, hay, and wages cost a lot of money.”

“Tsk tsk. You fool.”

“Hmm?”

“Of course the Heavenly Friends Alliance has money. With all those powerful sects gathered, how could they not?”

“That may be, but...”

“And it doesn’t cost as much money as you think.”

“Hmm? What do you mean?”

The speaker chuckled.

“Hey, you. Honestly, hasn’t the Heavenly Friends Alliance done a lot for us? They saved Yangtze river lands, which were falling apart. Didn’t they also stop those Demonic Cult bastards from moving north?”

“What obvious words are you saying? Who in Yangtze river isn’t grateful to the Heavenly Friends Alliance? Not only that, they saved people in the Yangtze River region where the pirates and the Four Sects Alliance ran rampant with nowhere to go.”

“Right, right. But if they take full wages and sell goods for full price, what kind of people are they?”

“So?”

“The workers all say they’ll only take half wages, and Mr. Jang and Mr. Yu sell without making profit except for the workers’ wages.”

“Haha. Really? They’re thoughtful people. So? Does Mount Hua like that?”

“Would they like it? It caused an uproar.”

“...Why another uproar?”

“I heard elder Hyun Jong, the sect leader of Mount Hua, went mad asking why they weren’t properly charging money. He said they should take full wages and proper profit.”

“Hoo... As expected of Mount Hua’s sect leader.”

“Exactly. So the workers begged the sect leader by grabbing his pants leg.”

“What, what did they say?”

“They said if they took full wages and profit, they’d be beaten to death by other people in the Yangtze river. Honestly, that’s not wrong. I wouldn’t have stayed quiet either.”

“Hahaha. That’s really funny. Having to beg not to take money.”

“The sect leader said absolutely no, but in the end, he couldn’t break the people’s will. They said they wouldn’t work if paid full price, but people urgently need work. What can you do? Even a sage-like person has things they can’t control.”

“If you think positively, we Yangtze river people have beaten the sage?”

“You could say that.”

The two looked at each other and chuckled.

As Mount Hua and the Heavenly Friends Alliance began residing in the Yangtze River region, the dying city started to revive. With not just one but several major sects residing there, even petty crimes and accidents disappeared like melting snow.

“By the way, did you bring this up to tell such obvious stories?”

“Oh dear, I digressed. So, did you not see it?”

“What exactly did you say I didn’t see? Did something happen at that manor?”

“No, for a few days now, strange light has been leaking from the manor every night. Did you really not see it?”

“I go to sleep as soon as the sun sets...”

“Tsk tsk tsk. Make sure to see it tonight. I’ve watched for days, and when the night deepens, suddenly a dazzling purple light flashes from the manor. My goodness, when that light bursts out, a thick fragrance that seems otherworldly also comes out... I’ve never seen such a sight in my life.”

“Really?”

“Do you think I’d joke after eating expensive food? You can check it out tonight.”

“Right. If true, it’s really amazing. What on earth is happening at that manor? Purple light and fragrance...”

The man, slightly tilting his head, suddenly clapped his hands.

“Could it be... that the Mount Hua person is ascending to immortality?”

“Hey, you! No matter how great those people are, ascending to immortality isn’t that easy!”

“No, no. Honestly, who in the world can ascend to immortality? Only those with the aura of a sage can do it, right?”

“What does that have to do with this?”

“Think about it. Would that Wudang sect leader ascend? Would that damned Shaolin abbot attain enlightenment?”

“...”

“Is there anyone else in the world called a sage besides elder Hyun Jong, the sect leader of Mount Hua? I wouldn’t be surprised if he ascended tomorrow...”

“Ah, no. Elder Hyun Jong is indeed a great Taoist, but... that’s another story...”

“What? Are you disrespecting Elder Hyun Jong now?”

The moment those words escaped, passersby all turned to look at them.

“Who?”

“Who the hell dared disrespect Elder Hyun Jong?”

“What crazy bastard dared do that in here!”

Before the fierce gazes could fall, several strong men rolled up their sleeves and approached. Seeing this, the man panicked and shouted.

“No! No! It’s a misunderstanding! How could that be! Elder Hyun Jong of Mount Hua is the most honorable person in the world! Yes! Yes!”

As his desperate screams to avoid being beaten echoed through the street, the Hyun Jong they praised was literally on the verge of ascending.

“Ughhh.”

Thud.

Hyun Jong collapsed onto the floor.

“I’m... dying...”

“Sect leader! Are you alright?”

“Ugh...”

From Hyun Jong’s face, now darkened, came continuous groans of pain. Beside him, Hyun Sang and Hyun Young lay unconscious.

“What did I... want to enjoy wealth and glory for...”

“H-here’s some water, sect leader!”

“Ugh...”

Groans kept escaping from Hyun Jong’s mouth.

‘He unnecessarily raised my internal qi.’

That damned guy kept feeding him spiritual pills for health, so I thought he cared about the sect leader. But he’s using it all up here, so frugally!

“The thing is sect leader... it’s already the fifth day.”

“...It should be almost done.”

Hyun Jong nodded and stared at the door from which white steam continuously escaped.

At that moment.

Flash!

A dazzling purple light burst out from the tightly closed door.

“It’s done!”

At the same time, before the loud voice inside could fade, someone kicked the door open and came out.

Bang!

“Ugh!”

Hyun Jong stared blankly at Chung Myung who kicked the door open. Carrying a large bundle bigger than his body on his back, he was exhaling purple steam from his mouth.

“After refining a few times, your skill has improved! I thought all Heavenly Violet Pills were the same.”

“Ugh. Chung Myung, is it done?”

At those words, Chung Myung tapped the bundle on his back.

“Perfect! This Heavenly Violet Pill will be much more effective than before. Hehe!”

“...Then that’s a relief.”

We worked those old guys day and night for five days; it better be effective... damn it.

“Hehehehe. Let’s see if anyone can complain after taking this. They’re all dead, bastards!”

Chung Myung chuckled and swiftly moved.

“Ch-Chung Myung...”

Hyun Jong, staring blankly at his rapidly retreating back, weakly lowered his half-stretched hand. Then he slowly turned his head to look at the elders.

“...Everyone, you’ve worked hard.”

“...Damn.”

“...”

Faint curses echoed softly along with the subtle fragrance of the Heavenly Violet Pill.

“Have you swept it all up?”

“No. This... I need to sweep a little more...”

“Come out. Out! It’s time!”

“Already?”

The person sweeping the path between the pavilions saw the rising sun and quickly stepped aside to the roadside. In their tense view, that strange spectacle unfolded once again.

Bang! Bang!

The doors of the pavilions, silent as a mouse all night, opened simultaneously as if on cue. Those sweeping the path swallowed their dry saliva upon seeing this.

Soon, people staggered out through the open doors.

There was no sign of life at all. These figures, better described as corpses or ghosts rather than people, walked out listlessly.

“Oh dear...”

Those sweeping the path shuddered at the sight.

Though they had seen this scene daily recently, they still couldn’t get used to it. How could anyone adapt to a sight like corpses walking around?

Those who moved sluggishly, looking like half-cooked seaweed, were driven toward the training ground.

“It even feels like their glow has faded, it’s creepy.”

It wasn’t like this at first.

At first, those heading to the training ground were too lively, which was a problem. They pointed fingers at each other and, in severe cases, even fought and tangled up.

But recently, everyone moved toward the training ground like cattle being dragged to the slaughterhouse, half out of their minds and moving mindlessly. It’s more astonishing that they go reluctantly despite hating it so much.

How long will this continue?

My calves ache; I can’t live like this.

Of course, those watching this scene also struggled, but no matter what, it couldn’t compare to the suffering of those who had become like that.

The only thought filling the minds of those heading to the training groundno, the slaughterhousewas one thing.

I feel like I’m going to die...

I wish I could sleep just one more hour...

Can a real person not die doing this? Really?

They had heard countless times that if a real war broke out, they wouldn’t sleep properly for ten days, or even a whole month.

But few had seriously thought about what that meant until they experienced it themselves.

At least we get some sleep...

How can we keep doing it like this?

Now, feelings toward each other, anger at the sect leaders pushing themnone of that remained. All that was left was the primal desire to somehow close their eyes and rest. At least...

“I will die, young lord.”

“...It seems worse than during Plum Blossom Island?”

“You speak carelessly. It’s disrespectful to those who died there.”

“...I’m sorry.”

Namgung, having briefly experienced something similar, were definitely a bit more composed than the others.

Which made it all the more irritating.

If there were no comparisons, they might have cursed openly, saying they didn’t understand why they trained like this. But since that damn Namgung showed even a faint sign of vigor, they could see the effect of this training with their own eyes.

So they couldn’t openly curse but still suffered the pain, creating a frustrating situation. Moreover...

“Ugh, damn it. Just kill me cleanly.”

“Don’t speak carelessly. If Chung Myung heard, he might really do that.”

“It’s better to just die, what is this?”

“Isn’t it giving you what you want?”

“Huh?”

“If we keep enduring this, we’ll eventually die.”

Those around looked weakly at Mount Hua, who had spent a long time with Chung Myung. One question arose.

But why don’t those guys get tired?

They say that’s them being tired?

Do Mount Hua guys chew on century-old snow ginseng as snacks? They say Mount Hua is a famous mountain, but maybe all the herbs there are spiritual herbs?

But those guys only eat meat?

Is that okay for Taoists to do that?

Of course, Tang Family and Green Forest also suffered heavy blows, but the most ridiculous in this situation were none other than Beast Palace and Ice Palace.

How could this happen...

Beast Palace and Ice Palace were not lacking in realism. Outwardly, for pride, they often said they could stand against the famous powerful sects of Central Plains, but they knew their martial prowess was not on par with the Nine Great Sects or the Five Great Families.

But that was only in terms of ‘martial prowess.’

What firmly supported them was their pride in their physical strength and mental fortitude, forged by battling harsh and rugged nature.

But after experiencing it firsthand, they realized how futile that pride was.

I thought at least our mental strength... our mental strength wouldn’t fail...

Of course, if Chung Myung heard that, he would have retorted, “Mental strength is for training; in real combat, you only get out what you put in! Do slackers suddenly get strength when a knife is at their throat? Huh?” But what is obvious to some is not obvious to others.

Some bore hardship, some hardship and wounds, others subtle resentment, but regardless of their inner feelings, all arrived at the training ground with lifeless faces.

He’s already out there.

Let’s rest even for a moment, you damn bastards.

How does that guy get more energetic day by day? How?

The moment they spotted Chung Myung already settled in the training ground, everyone’s faces twisted in horror.

It was incomprehensible. Logically, those fighting against them should be at least as exhausted, if not more.

Indeed, the faces of Tang Gunak and Meng So standing beside Chung Myung showed clear signs of fatigue. Though they kept a straight posture before others, the roughened skin and shadows on their faces were undeniable.

But...

Why is that guy so shiny?

Chung Myung’s face was glowing. He even looked more vigorous now than when they first started training.

Is he really a monster?

Is he sucking off qi or something?

I’m scared he’ll appear in my dreams, really...

Life is a comedy from afar and a tragedy up close.

The Heavenly Friends Alliance members who had chuckled watching Mount Hua suffer from Chung Myung’s mischief were now paying a terrible price for their laughter.

But why is he settled like that today?

What is he going to make us do this time... Please, just let us do what we were doing... Please...

Just then, everyone looked anxiously at Chung Myung and the sect leaders standing beside him.

“Ahem.”

Uncharacteristically, Chung Myung covered his mouth with his fist and politely cleared his throat.

The disciples of Mount Hua recognized this as Chung Myung’s typical gesture when starting something grand. Anxiety clouded their faces like storm clouds.

“Everyone...”

Chung Myung scanned the gathered people and began to speak.

“You all have no spirit at all.”

Hearing this, they all bristled and glared at Chung Myung. Who was responsible for this, and he said it so calmly...

“Tsk tsk tsk.”

But Chung Myung openly clicked his tongue as if he didn’t care.

“Why are you already out of energy and panting? When I was your age, huh?”

“You’re the youngest one here, you bastard!”

“Ah, right.”

Startled by Jo Gul’s outburst, Chung Myung nodded.

“Anyway!”

Chung Myung looked at everyone with a disdainful expression and continued.

“Well... it’s not because I pity seeing you panting from exhaustion, but... if you put in some effort, your training efficiency will improve... and with better training efficiency, it’s easier to apply it. Right? That’s why...”

What is he saying?

Where’s that barking coming from?

What nonsense is he trying to make us do now?

As Chung Myung awkwardly rambled on, Tang Gunak finally sighed deeply and interrupted.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

“Yes?”

“...Hurry up. It’s hard to just stand in here.”

Chung Myung silently pouted, then grabbed the bundle he had set down behind and began to unwrap it.

“Elders, please distribute these to the kids.”

“Understood.”

“One pill each! If you get caught hiding them, your hand gets cut off!”

The elders shook their heads and approached to take the pills inside the bundle. Then they distributed one pill each to the disciples.

“...What is this?”

“What are they giving?”

Those standing at the back, looking puzzled, hesitated but moved forward.

It seemed like something was happening, but with so many people, it was hard to figure out exactly what.

What is this?

A Beast Palace disciple tilted his head, watching an elder from Beast Palace approaching him.

Doesn’t seem like anything special.

If it were something great, they wouldn’t include Beast Palace and Ice Palace, right?

Before even discussing discrimination between Central Plains and the outer regions, Beast Palace and Ice Palace were not qualified to be included in the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s merit distribution. So it was best not to expect much...

“Take this.”

“What...”

The elder who approached him placed a pill awkwardly onto his outstretched hand without a word.

“What is this...”

The Beast Palace disciple, seeing the slightly purple pill, was about to tilt his head but flinched as if struck by lightning.

The indescribably pure fragrance wafting to his nose explained more clearly than words what it was.

“A, Spiritual... Spiritual Pill?”

Confirming the radiant purple pill with his eyes once more, his hands began to tremble. Fearing he might drop it, he quickly cupped it with both hands and looked around in shock.

“Oh my god. What is this?”

“Spiritual pill?”

“W-why are they suddenly giving this? And is it even allowed to have so many spiritual pills? Isn’t this cheating?”

Others reacted similarly.

What exactly is a spiritual pill? Even within a sect, only a tiny amount is made, and only core talents or those recognized for great achievements get to see them.

Of course, those who saved the country in a past life might find spiritual like Human Shaped Tuberous Roots or Millennial Old Snow Ginseng while climbing mountains, but such occurrences are rare, maybe once every ten years.

Even in Shaolin, the richest sect in the world, it is said that many never see a Great Vitality Pill or even a Small Vitality Pill in their lifetime.

But is it really possible to distribute such spiritual pills like snacks to a house dog?

Fortunately, there was someone here to answer their questions.

Everyone’s gaze reflexively turned sharply toward Tang Pae, the young lord of Sichuan Tang Family. He was staring blankly at the spiritual pill. After a moment of daze, he brought it close to his nose and sniffed, then murmured.

“...It’s a real spiritual pill. And a top-grade one.”

The surroundings fell silent as if a mouse had died.

‘No, what kind of spiritual pills...’

There were too many things that didn’t make sense logically.

First of all, how on earth were these many spiritual pills made? But that was a problem that could be set aside for now. The more important question was why they were distributing these pills to them.

‘And those were spiritual pills that even Tang Family’s young lord evaluated so highly...’

Of course, the size was a bit small.

Generally, spiritual pills are said to be at least the size of a chestnut, but the pills in their hands now were about the size of a bean.

But so what?

Whether bean-sized or not, if it’s a top-grade spiritual pill, even one the size of a grain of rice can cause a bloodbath the moment it spreads in the murim world.

Martial artists risk their lives for martial prowess. If there’s any way to increase their power, they won’t hesitate to fight with swords. So whenever they hear that spirituals have appeared somewhere, they all clutch their weapons and rush to where the rumors came from. Even knowing their chances of obtaining the spiritual are slim, they can’t give up that small hope.

‘But to just scatter such spiritual pills like this?’

Everyone looked at Chung Myung with faces full of disbelief.

Though divided into small portions, the amount distributed to so many people would have been enough to give each Mount Hua disciple a full-sized spiritual pill and still have some left.

What sect leader in the world would share the spiritual pills meant for their own disciples with others?

“I-Is it really the spiritual pills?”

So even knowing, they couldn’t help but doubt.

“C-Could it be poison?”

“Why would they feed us poison!”

“We’re all weak lately, maybe they’re giving us poison to get us moving? Like, work hard if you don’t want to die...”

Those who were about to refute that looked back at Chung Myung with uneasy expressions. It was absurd, but with that person, it might really be possible...

“Tang family’s young lord said they’re spiritual pills!”

Ah, that’s right.

“No, wait. It’s Tang Family. They say those people even consume poison pills like spirituals? Then poison pills could also be called spiritual pills, right?”

“Huh?”

Thinking there might be some logic, everyone’s gaze turned back to Tang Pae. Then Tang Pae protested as if wronged.

“No matter what, do you think I can’t distinguish poison pills from spiritual pills? And the rumor that Tang people consume poison like spirituals is all nonsense!”

“...Is that true?”

“Then do sword sects eat swords instead of spiritual pills?”

“Then what about the Heavenly Poison Pills?”

“That’s a different matter! If just eating poison could increase inner power, we’d have more power than Shaolin! We’d be beating up Namgung too.”

“Why are we suddenly...”

Everyone nodded, clearly convinced by that explanation.

“So this really is spiritual pills...”

The doubt was resolved, but a bigger question arose. Sending him looks of disbelief yet seeking explanation, Chung Myung indifferently gestured.

“If you’ve all received them, then eat.”

“...”

“Don’t make it complicated by sharing them around; eat them all while the elders guard you and then cultivate your qi.”

“No...”

One from the Beast Palace looked at Chung Myung with a dazed expression and asked.

“I-is it really okay to eat this?”

“Would I have distributed something you can’t eat?”

“That’s not what I meant...”

Would everyone here be so modest as to just look at the spiritual pills in their hands without eating them?

They too thought that if the spiritual pills were right in front of them, they’d swallow them first and worry about the consequences later.

But all precious items require paying a fair price and going through a process to obtain. Yet these spiritual pills, which they never even dreamed of, just ended up in their hands.

Because of this, no one knew what to do or how to act.

If alone, they’d swallow without hesitation, but with so many others in the same situation, it was awkward to be the first to eat the spiritual pills.

“Ah, eat! Can’t you hear? These guys can’t even eat when given?”

Those holding the spiritual pills nervously exchanged glances at the scolding from the front. But no one could give a clear answer, so their gaze eventually turned to the sect leaders of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

A brief chuckle escaped from Meng So. The robust Palace people were acting like restless puppies, anxiously looking this way, which was ridiculous.

‘Was it this bad?’

This reaction wasn’t just because they received spirituals. His archers fundamentally couldn’t understand receiving anything from Central Plains people without any cost.

Though they had talked about cooperating with Central Plains and nesting under the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s roof, the distrust in their hearts hadn’t dissipated.

In fact, the one who should have resolved this issue was none other than Meng So. He had been the most proactive about exchanges with Central Plains and should have taken the initiative first...

Meng So glanced at Chung Myung, frowning deeply as if annoyed.

‘It’s just his expression.’

Now he somewhat understood Chung Myung. This person, awkward in dealing with others, sharpens his edge when embarrassed or receiving flattery.

“Ahem.”

Meng So cleared his throat briefly and spoke.

“Those spiritual pills are...”

What should he say? Maeng So took in everyone looking at him and continued.

“They are spiritual pills prepared by the Heavenly Friends Alliance for you.”

This was the proper way to say it. It was certainly not wrong.

These spiritual pills are the essence of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, made from the ice crystals of the Northern Sea, the medicinal herb of the Beast Palace, and the efforts of Mount Hua.

If one stretches it, the financial power of Namgung family, the Green Forest’s facilitation of trade across the land, and even Tang Family’s alchemy techniques contributed.

No matter how great Mount Hua is, the Heavenly Violet Pill cannot be made by their power alone.

“So don’t look at it with such eyes. You are simply enjoying what you rightfully deserve.”

This is probably the official stance of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and Chung Myung’s position to break down walls among its members.

When one side gives something to another, there is inevitably a giver and a receiver. Chung Myung does not want that. Such a small difference in position eventually divides into superior and inferior.

Those belonging to the Heavenly Friends Alliance receive equal treatment regardless of their sect. Isn’t that the principle Chung Myung newly established?

So for Meng So, who must uphold this great principle, the only answer he could give was this. But...

“However...”

Meng So paused, glanced at Chung Myung, and smiled faintly.

‘I don’t necessarily have to follow your words, right?’

Since Chung Myung said there is no superior or inferior in the Heavenly Friends Alliance, using that contradiction isn’t necessarily wrong.

“Stripped of the fancy packaging, these spiritual pills were made by Mount Hua and distributed to you.”

“No, that’s...”

Just as Chung Myung was about to speak, Meng So quickly continued, cutting him off.

“There’s no need to refuse what is given. It’s not something small enough to refuse under the pretext of spiritual pills or whatever. But to greedily accept what is given without gratitude is not what a person with manners would do.”

Chung Myung glared at Meng So, but Meng So cleanly ignored the gaze.

“So you should be grateful to Mount Hua, who stayed up nights making the spiritual pills and selflessly gave them to you, and take them with that gratitude.”

Chung Myung’s eyes blazed.

“Ahem.”

Under a gaze as if he might devour him whole, Meng So quietly averted his eyes. Then Tang Gunak stepped up to support Meng So.

“The same goes for Tang Family.”

At those words, all Tang people stared at Tang Gunak.

“Some ill-natured people say they feed others well to exploit them, but everyone knows it’s not as easy as it sounds.”

“...”

“No need to say thank you. No, don’t try to excuse yourself with such easy words. The way to pay the price for these spiritual pills is both difficult and simple: to make sure that in the future, the spiritual pills you receive are judged to have been worth it. That will be enough. No one expects more than that.”

“...”

“However... be sure to have gratitude for the alliance leader and Mount Hua elders who collapsed exhausted making these spiritual pills for us. That is separate from this.”

The Tang people felt a weight in their hearts and nodded.

“Is that how it will be?”

Chung Myung gritted his teeth and asked, and Tang Gunak shrugged.

“Then what should I say? That it’s nothing?”

“It wasn’t actually that difficult.”

“You’re saying comforting words. Don’t you know that the moment you say that, we become fools who couldn’t even do that easy thing?”

“...”

“To avoid becoming incompetent fools, we have to speak up. Surely you don’t mean we should take criticism without even defending ourselves?”

“Ugh...”

Chung Myung groaned.

“No, that’s not what I meant...”

Then, as he casually turned his head, he flinched and stepped back.

It was because those who looked like zombies just moments ago were now staring at him with bright eyes, which was quite overwhelming.

Flustered, Chung Myung broke down.

“Uh, um...”

“Ah, really.”

At that moment, a savior appeared.

“This isn’t puppy training! Are you going to hold a ritual in front of the spiritual pills?”

Everyone’s gaze turned to the loud voice. Im So-Byeong was already sitting on the floor, having thrown away his fan and frowning.

“Whether you thank or repay the favor is up to you. First, show some manners. Once the honored guests finish their turn, let’s eat. Okay? My legs are shaking and I’m about to die. Is this the time to fuss over this?”

“Uh...”

That was certainly true.

“If you’re not going to eat, clear it away. I’m going to eat.”

Im So-Byeong immediately stuffed the Heavenly Violet Pill into his mouth and sat cross-legged.

Isn’t the hardest part always the first?

Once Im So-Byeong broke the ice, others also sat cross-legged one by one and pushed the Heavenly Violet Pill into their mouths.

“By the way, Chung Myung.”

Jo Gul raised his hand as if he didn’t understand something, but Chung Myung waved it off as if annoyed to listen.

“You all just eat it.”

“It’s not that...”

“Ah, just eat it. No need to save some for others. Everyone eats the same. Eating more won’t cause any harm.”

“No, that’s not what I meant!”

“Huh?”

Jo Gul held up his share of the Heavenly Violet Pill, his face twisted.

“Our share seems a bit smaller than the others’?”

“...”

“Are you sure they’re all the same?”

“...”

“Don’t cheat us, just tell the truth. If you say it now, I’ll go easy on you.”

Chung Myung, veins bulging on his forehead, rolled up his sleeves for the first time in a while.

It is quiet.

It is rare enough to see a large number of martial artists gathered in a vast training ground, but seeing so many of them simultaneously sitting cross-legged and practicing qi cultivation is an even rarer sight beyond comparison.

‘How fascinating.’

Tang Gunak looked at the Heavenly Friends Alliance members practicing qi cultivation with a renewed sense of wonder.

To those unfamiliar with the life of martial artists, it might just seem fascinating, but for those who understand the nature of martial artists, this scene holds a special significance.

Basically, qi cultivation is usually done secretly away from others’ eyes. Why? It’s very simple. Martial artists become most vulnerable while cultivating qi.

Even those who can shatter rocks with a single strike or leap over rivers in a single step can fall into a trance from a child’s kick while cultivating qi.

Therefore, martial artists rarely show their qi cultivation to even their closest kin. Like a beast seeking a secret den before giving birth, hiding their vulnerable moments from others is almost instinctual.

But here, countless people are cultivating qi openly in the training ground where everyone watches.

‘Because there was an order?’

No way. Normally, even with orders from Tang Gunak or Chung Myung, they would have sought a quiet place to cultivate qi while holding their spiritual pills. At least, they would have done it in their own quarters.

That they are cultivating qi so casually in this vast training ground means they no longer perceive those around them as threats.

‘Who knows.’

Tang Gunak shook his head.

The word ‘trust’ is truly a strange thing.

No one denies the importance of mutual trust. Leaders of sects all wish their disciples to build trust among themselves. Hence, they establish hierarchies through numerous etiquettes and enforce many rules to fairly judge disputes among disciples.

Yet, trust is not easily built. People may appear to trust each other outwardly but choose differently at critical moments.

‘If I had done the same at the Tang family, could everyone really cultivate qi like this?’

He already knows the answer.

Therefore, it was uncertain. All they had done when gathered was fight fiercely, play tricks on each other, and unite against the sect leaders.

Was there even room for human interaction? Then what did they trust to turn their backs on each other and reveal their most vulnerable parts without hesitation?

Tang Gunak glanced at Chung Myung.

Every time, he realized anew that Mount Hua’s Sword Saint sees something he cannot. When will he be able to see what that person sees?

“Why is that?”

“I’m just a bit grumpy.”

“Huh?”

“...”

“Your temper has worsened. Or maybe it’s better than before?”

“Ugh.”

Tang Gunak shook his head vigorously. If only he could do something about that temperament, it would be perfect.

‘Anyway, it’s a good thing.’

Though he had traveled a long way, this scene seemed to tell him that the ‘relationships’ of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, which Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had worked so hard to build, were gradually being established.

“But you managed to make Spiritual pills for all those kids.”

“Ugh. Well...”

Chung Myung scratched his chin.

“Actually, it’s not a proper Spiritual pill. Every Spiritual pill has an appropriate dosage. But due to lack of ingredients, I had to reduce the size, so its effect is less than one-fifth compared to taking a proper Heavenly Violet Pill.”

“So it has at least one-fifth effect?”

“Hmm... It might be even less than that.”

“Did you say this Heavenly Violet Pill was made better than before?”

“Yes. So we can at least expect one-fifth of the effect.”

Chung Myung sighed deeply.

“They fight well only if they take it well.”

“You always do things others can’t even dream of, yet criticize yourself for being insufficient. You’re too harsh on yourself.”

“It’s not that I’m harsh on myself, but our situation is just harsh. Still, we can’t just die quietly because we’re outmatched, can we?”

“Hard to argue with that.”

Most people focus on the results of their actions. But Chung Myung only checks how close he is to his goals.

Watching him like that sometimes sends chills down one’s spine.

Tang Gunak thought it was fortunate not to have met Chung Myung as an enemy.

“By the way...”

A sigh escaped his lips.

“It’s the best thing from the Alliance’s perspective, but not entirely welcome from my side.”

“Hmm? Why?”

“Even if it’s one-fifth, a Spiritual pill is a Spiritual pill, right? Especially one-fifth of Mount Hua’s Heavenly Violet Pill is equivalent to a secret Spiritual pill of most sects...”

Tang Gunak groaned uncharacteristically.

“Their internal qi and stamina will increase, and I have to face such guys again.”

While it was desirable for the kids to bond and grow stronger through Spiritual pills, from the perspective of controlling them, it wasn’t entirely welcome.

He was already exhausted dealing with kids who hadn’t taken Spiritual pills, sometimes collapsing on the ground to sleep; how could he handle those who had taken them?

“Oh, you’re worrying too much. I prepared one separately for the clan head.”

“Huh?”

Chung Myung rummaged through his pouch and took out a well-packaged Spiritual pill.

“I prepared the most potent ones for the Family lord, the Palace lord, and So-Baek.”

Tang Gunak stared intently at the Spiritual pill in Chung Myung’s hand. It was a proper Heavenly Violet Pill, much larger than those given to the kids.

“This...”

“Don’t tell me to give more to the kids unnecessarily.”

“I would have said that for the sake of pride before, but now I’m not in a position to care about such things.”

“I’ll take care of this, so please take it separately tonight. Also, I prepared some smaller ones for the elders.”

Chung Myung was certainly meticulous in these matters.

“Wait a minute. You said for me and the two palace lords? Then what about... the Green Forest King?”

“Shh.”

Chung Myung looked around and whispered with his finger to his lips.

“I prepared it well; take it somewhere no one sees you. Got it?”

“...”

“Because a greedy Evil Sect bastard would bother us if he found out about this.”

Tang Gunak turned his head and looked at Im So-Byeong cultivating qi.

Im So-Byeong smiled oddly even while cultivating qi, seemingly happy to have received the Spiritual pill, which stirred a wave of pity in Tang Gunak’s heart.

‘Born in the Evil Sect by chance...’

No, more precisely, someone born in the Evil Sect who met Chung Myung...

Knowing Chung Myung’s deep-rooted and incurable hatred of the Evil Sect, Tang Gunak silently tucked the received Spiritual pill deep inside his pouch.

“...Understood.”

“Yes, hehe.”

Chung Myung turned his head and looked at those cultivating qi.

“It’s about time to finish.”

A faint smile appeared on his lips.

Wooooooong.

Namgung Do-Wei, who had been sinking deeper and deeper inside, pushed his qi up to the Hundred Union blood-point. The qi surged upward through his meridians like a soaring dragon ascending.

Kuuuuung!

At the same time, a loud bell-like sound echoed inside his head.

Black dead blood flowed from Namgung Do-Wei’s nose and mouth. It wasn’t an internal wound but the impurities damaged by long training being expelled from his body along with the dead blood.

‘Again!’

Kwahaaaaaang!

The qi surged again, hitting his Hundred Union blood-point with a brilliant explosion. The energy spreading throughout his body created an indescribable vitality.

“Hoo...”

Namgung Do-Wei slowly opened his eyes. At the same time, he saw Chung Myung standing ahead, arms behind his back, watching them.

‘It had to be now.’

The overflowing energy inside him seemed to say that if he had taken this Spiritual pill before starting training, he wouldn’t have achieved this much effect.

The Spiritual pill’s effect raises the internal qi. But more importantly, the moment one takes the Spiritual pill, they gain the chance to use a great qi they normally couldn’t.

How one utilizes this once-in-a-lifetime opportunity is crucial.

Those who fight endlessly and realize their limits instinctively learn how to use that qi.

‘I’ve crossed one wall.’

Without the Spiritual pill’s help, who knows how long it would have taken. The Spiritual pill given at the right time drastically shortened that time. It was such an achievement that the newly accumulated internal qi felt insignificant.

He rose with a confident face. Pure light poured from his eyes.

Not long after he stood, Tang Pae also rose from his seat. His face was not much different from Namgung Do-Wei’s.

One by one.

Those sitting cross-legged began to stand up one by one. Before an hour had passed, all who had been cultivating qi stood and lined up.

Their gaze naturally turned to Chung Myung standing ahead.

They didn’t know what to say, but at this moment, they clearly knew who they should look at.

Receiving everyone’s gaze, Chung Myung nodded deeply.

Under his sharp gaze, everyone showed confident smiles.

Chung Myung spoke.

“First of all...”

“...”

“Ugh!”

“...Huh?”

Suddenly, Chung Myung gagged, grabbed his nose, and hurriedly stepped back.

“...Go wash up, please...”

Everyone flinched and checked themselves. Waste excreted from their pores had stuck and clumped, turning their clothes pitch black.

“Uh...”

“Ugh. What is this smell?”

“Ugh!”

Only then did those who smelled the foul stench coming from their bodies start groaning here and there.

Tang Gunak solemnly held his nose with one hand and raised his voice loudly.

“...Go to the river. Quickly!”

“...”

“Ah, just go!”

Those who had expected something great ran awkwardly toward the Yangtze River.

For several days afterward, people living near the Yangtze River suffered collective stomach pains, causing officials to investigate whether it was a sign of plague, a minor issue...

Regardless, the Yangtze River just flowed steadily onward.

“Is it finished?”

“J-just a little left...”

“No, what is so important about sweeping the yard that you can’t finish on time every single time? Isn’t this laziness too much?”

“Oh, don’t even mention it. If we rush to finish on time, we get scolded. The elder of Mount Hua comes and calls us food parasites and gets so angry!”

“...You mean Elder Hyun Young?”

“I don’t know the exact characters, but that strict person...”

“I see.”

“Yes. Anyway, that person said that since many people come and go in the manor, if it’s not kept clean, those who see it will look down on those gathered here...”

“...Then sweep it.”

“Yes?”

“...”

The most feared figure among those working in the manor was neither Hyun Jong, the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, nor Chung Myung, the fiend of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Hyun Jong is so gentle to those untrained in martial arts that he might as well be live bait... no, well... anyway, he is endlessly warm-hearted. Chung Myung does not harass civilians or martial artists indiscriminately, but since there are those who must be troubled first, he does not turn his attention to others.

To them, the grim reaper-like figure is none other than Hyun Young, the Finance Hall leader of Mount Hua.

“Not all Taoists have... good personalities...”

“Hey, you! How dare you say such a thing out loud! You want to be struck by lightning?”

“S-sorry.”

“Say such things quietly, where others can’t hear you.”

“...Yes.”

The one who gave advice suddenly turned his head sharply to check the sky.

“It’s time! Step aside quickly!”

“Yes!”

The two hurriedly stepped aside. It was time for the daily routine to begin.

‘Ugh, really.’

‘Seeing that sight again today.’

As if by agreement, the doors of the pavilions all opened simultaneously, and the two tensed up, swallowing dry saliva.

But the scene that unfolded today was distinctly different from usual.

“Hurrah!”

“It’s morning!”

“Let’s go! To the training ground!”

Before the people could even be seen, loud voices burst out. Then, from the opened doors, Heavenly Friends Alliance warriors armed with weapons poured out in a rush.

“Huh? What, what’s this?”

Those who usually shuffled out like half-dead bodies now ran out energetically, startling the workers who widened their eyes.

‘What is this?’

‘Am I seeing things?’

But it seemed they were not mistaken. Those who came out stretched vigorously and smiled broadly.

“Ah! How can my body feel this light!”

“Now I understand why everyone goes crazy over the spiritual pills!”

“I could fight all day!”

“Let’s go!”

“I’m first!”

The crowd started running toward the training ground all at once. The two pressed against the wall blinked as those who passed by like the wind.

“Hello!”

“Yes?”

“Thank you for your hard work!”

“Th-thank you...”

Those who had never made eye contact before now greeted with lively voices. Of course, they passed by quickly right after greeting.

“So what kind of training are they doing today?”

“Maybe they’re sparring with the sect leaders?”

“Really? Finally, the day of revenge!”

“Eh. But they’re sect leaders, so ‘revenge’ sounds a bit off.”

“If that’s the case, why are they running so fast?”

Leaving a huge dust cloud, the warriors disappeared toward the training ground. The two pressed against the roadside looked at each other in disbelief.

“You...”

“Yes?”

“...I think we have to sweep this again from the start.”

Looking at the messy path, he sighed deeply and bowed his head.

Those who joined the Heavenly Friends Alliance all experienced many changes.

Those who lived only within one sect had to coexist with others, so change was inevitable, not optional.

However, among the many in the Heavenly Friends Alliance, the one who changed the most surprisingly was not a martial artist.

“Hey!”

A loud scolding came from Lady Chu.

“I told you not to lift the pot with just two people! It’s heavier than you think and accidents happen! You need three people together!”

“Yes, madam!”

“Is the chicken still not done?”

“Oh dear! It needs to be boiled a bit more. The pot is so big...”

“The fire is weak! Quickly bring more firewood. There should be new firewood in the backyard!”

“Yes! Yes! I’m going now!”

Lady Chu looked around intensely to make sure nothing was missed.

“M-madam! They are asking for more rice.”

“We just served a pot.”

“Oh dear. Today seems to be a special day; as soon as the pot is put down, it’s all gone! It’s like some greedy fish have latched on...”

“Rice cakes! The rice still needs a bit more time, so first bring out the rice cakes we planned as dessert. If they have something to eat, they’ll wait a bit! They’re in the basket over there!”

“Yes, yes! I’ll bring them right away!”

“One pot of rice... no, bring two more! No, just bring three pots! Quickly!”

“Yes, madam!”

Lady Chu, who gave orders until her voice was hoarse, wiped the sweat dripping down her face with her sleeve due to the heat.

“What kind of chaos is this.”

Meal times in the manor always resembled a battlefield. But today seemed especially chaotic.

“Madam! There’s no rice!”

“Meat! What about meat? Meat!”

“Ah, I wish we had one more chicken.”

Mount Hua disciples poked their heads through the kitchen door and started crying like hungry baby birds... no, hungry grown birds.

Madam Chu shuddered at the sight. But before she could say anything, a hand from behind grabbed the collars of those nagging.

“I told you not to enter the kitchen!”

“S-sasuk, it’s not like that!”

“Oh dear, I can’t live like this! Why are you all like this!”

“No, Soso, we’re just too hungry...”

Baek Cheon and Tang Soso dragged the Mount Hua disciples away. Yoon Jong sighed and bowed his head toward Lady Chu.

“I’m sorry. They’re good-hearted kids but just a little, no, very foolish...”

“Ah, no. I’ll bring it quickly.”

“You can take your time.”

“Ah, sahyung! If you go slowly, they won’t finish eating within meal time...”

“Watch your mouth!”

Yoon Jong elbowed Jo Gul’s head, who was late to speak, and nodded awkwardly.

“Then.”

After the two left, Lady Chu wiped the sweat from her forehead again.

‘What happened?’

Usually, they ate a lot but had no vitality on their faces. Seeing them suddenly so excited to eat made her feel energized despite the hard work.

“Those greedy bastards sure eat well.”

At that moment, a voice from the side startled Lady Chu, who turned sharply. Hyun Young, radiating strictness, was walking over with a nonchalant expression.

“You’re here?”

“Today they’ll eat especially a lot. Work hard.”

“I’m the one who works hard? The cooks do.”

“You say that.”

Hyun Young frowned and suddenly handed something tucked under his arm to Madam Chu.

“Oh my! Hak!”

Lady Chu was surprised and took the child Hyun Young handed over.

“What about the child...”

“The child keeps crying and throwing tantrums; it’s so noisy! Calm him down for a bit!”

“Sorry...”

Lady Chu, checking the child’s condition with a sorry look, sighed deeply.

“...Elder, I told you, you don’t have to change the child’s cloth...”

“I did it because he cried so much, not because I like the child. Don’t worry about it.”

“Still...”

“No more talk... You! How dare you approach with a knife when there’s a child here!”

“S-sorry, Elder.”

A cook who was about to pass by thoughtlessly was startled by Hyun Young’s shout and stepped back. Of course, it was more Hyun Young’s fault for bringing a child into the dangerous kitchen, but no one dared to question that.

Moreover, everyone knew why Hyun Young insisted on bringing the child here, so no one could say anything.

After Lady Chu calmed and put the child to sleep, Hyun Young took the baby back with a nonchalant expression.

“It’s hard, isn’t it?”

“No, it’s not hard...”

“Work is naturally hard. If you get paid and it isn’t hard, then you’re a thief.”

Hyun Young muttered with a nonchalant face.

“But the pay for hard work belongs entirely to the worker. No one helps or owes anyone. Understand?”

“Yes, Elder.”

“Tsk.”

Hyun Young pulled the child closer, turned around, and suddenly scolded another cook.

“Hey! Clean up the spills on the floor properly!”

“Yes, yes! Sorry, Elder.”

“From tomorrow, the ingredients will come in double, so assign some people in the morning to help with cleaning.”

“Oh dear, Elder... We are too short-staffed. If we do that too, we...”

“When did I say not to hire more people? If you’re short, hire more. Talk and hire. Immediately!”

“Yes, yes! If you do that...”

As Hyun Young walked away, pointing out things here and there, a small smile appeared on Lady Chu’s lips.

‘Really...’

Though it was hard, it was truly fortunate to be able to come here amidst it all.

“Is the rice ready?”

“There’s no chicken here right now.”

“Ah! The Green Forest and Namgung kids are fighting over the meat! Meat! Please give us some meat first! Quickly!”

“...”

Lady Chu covered her face with her hands.

‘No. It doesn’t seem to be always like that.’

Those greedy bastards...

But soon Lady Chu raised her head sharply and shouted.

“I’ll give it to you, so get out of the kitchen! Quickly! I told you blocking the way makes it take longer!”

“Ah, no. We just...”

“Hurry!”

“Yes!”

Watching the heads quickly retreat, she shook her head.

“Is the chicken fully cooked?”

“Coming now, madam!”

“Quickly, quickly bring it!”

Her shoulders bounced vigorously as she rolled up her sleeves.

Chung Myung, watching the chaotic dining hall, tore off a chicken leg. The kids devoured one piece each and were so lively they were almost wild.

‘The effect is better than I thought?’

It seems that pushing people to their limits is the only way to make the medicine work properly.

People only appreciate what they have when it’s gone. Having realized the importance of stamina, which they usually didn’t care about, they will now train themselves.

“Well then, let’s see. We’ve done most of what we needed to do.”

The system is set.

So now, like Mount Hua in the past, they will start pushing themselves. To open a new waterway, you must dig the ground, but once opened, water flows continuously.

In other words, the long, rough, and arduous process is now over.

“Then it’s time to move on to the next stage.”

He nodded vigorously, stuffed the torn chicken leg into his mouth, and snapped his greasy fingers.

“Tell all the sect leaders to gather after the meal!”

Protests erupted here and there.

“Finish what’s in your mouth before you speak!”

“You’re flicking grease, you bastard! It’s disgusting.”

“...”

The kids seem way too energetic. Ugh.

An old man sits cross-legged with his eyes closed.

His white hair flows down to his waist, a long beard reaches his chest, his eyebrows are pure white, and he wears white robes covering his entire body. His appearance naturally evokes the phrase ‘immortal aura and Taoist spirit’.

Moreover, the old man exuded an aura not found in ordinary people, a noble atmosphere that naturally inspires reverence in those who observe him.

Even those who do not believe in immortals would have to acknowledge their existence upon seeing this old man.

The old man’s slightly faded lips parted gently, and a whispered incantation flowed out.

Countless flickering candles illuminated the dark room, and at their center, the immortal-like old man chanted the incantation.

The solemn atmosphere was broken by the sharp creak of an old hinge twisting as a door was opened.

Creeeeak.

The harsh sound mixed with the old man’s chanting, but he seemed unaware and continued his incantation without faltering.

Step. Step.

Clear footsteps grew steadily closer.

The peace and silence surrounding the old man faded with the approaching footsteps, and a low voice whispered into his ear.

“I’m back.”

Since the old man was the only one present, the words were surely directed at him. Yet strangely, he showed no particular reaction except stopping his chant, which he had been reciting almost like a song.

“It took longer than I thought. Damn sluggish worms.”

Each time the voice spoke, the candles in the room flickered wildly. Yet the old man’s calm heart remained unshaken.

“I killed him as ordered.”

A person’s death is never a trivial matter. Still, the old man’s expression remained unchanged in silence.

“But... it seems like a needless act. I almost didn’t have to go there.”

A slightly sharp emotion seeped from the voice.

“Even if I hadn’t gone, he would have died at the hands of the Central Plains guys.”

Instead of answering, the old man resumed his incantation. No voice or event seemed able to disturb his composure.

“By the hand of that Mount Hua guy.”

And at that moment, the old man’s seemingly endless chant began to fade.

When the old man closed his mouth firmly, a heavy silence fell over the room where the two men were. After a long pause, the old man finally spoke in a faint, murmuring voice.

“Mount Hua...”

He muttered softly with his eyes closed.

“A name I long for.”

“Hmph.”

The man standing behind the old man, Heaven Killer, frowned as he stared at the old man’s back.

“Still, it seems the word ‘Mount Hua’ reaches your ears.”

“Yes. A name I cherish. To think they still remain.”

A gentle smile formed on the old man’s lips.

“If possible, I’d like to see them once. How Mount Hua has changed now. I wish they were still as they were in the past. There aren’t many things left connected to my past.”

Heaven Killer’s mouth twisted slightly upon hearing those words.

“They haven’t changed much.”

“That’s good to hear.”

“There’s even someone like the Sword Saint.”

It was at that moment.

The old man’s head, which had been still until now, slowly turned back. At the same time, his tightly closed eyes opened.

The old man’s eyes were very different from those of ordinary people.

They were blood-red and pitch-black, as if mixed together, set like dots in his pure white exterior. The immortal saint-like appearance of the old man instantly transformed.

Anyone who saw this old man now would no longer think of an immortal. Eyes are the mirror of the soul, but in his eyes existed only deep, profound darkness.

“...What did you say?”

“Heh heh heh heh.”

Heaven Killer covered his mouth and laughed.

“Have you gone deaf now too?”

“I asked what you said.”

“You said you saw someone like the Sword Saint.”

“Sword Saint?”

The candles began to flicker. More precisely, the entire pavilion where the old man resided began to tremble.

“The Sword Saint you speak of, is it that cursed guy?”

“Is there another Sword Saint in the world?”

The old man’s calm hair began to stand on end. Watching this, Heaven Killer laughed heartily and shook his head.

“Calm down, great priest. It’s just a feeling. A rookie. Yes, just a rookie.”

“...”

“Well, who knows? In fifty years, he might become a real Sword Saint.”

“Impossible.”

The old man’s hair that had risen suddenly settled back down. Regaining composure, he spoke sharply.

“There cannot be another like him in this world.”

“...That is true.”

The old man sighed softly.

“Mount Hua has revived and is raising someone like Sword Saint is in it again.”

“There is one who stands out... but there are definitely others with potential. Annoying ones.”

“That means time has passed.”

Regret seeped into the old man’s voice.

“A hundred years is no short time. It’s enough for a sect whose foundation vanished to bloom again.”

“While we rot in here.”

At those words, the old man’s lips twitched slightly.

“Heaven Killer.”

“I know, so stop the old-fashioned talk. I’ve heard it for a hundred years and I’m sick of it.”

The old man quietly looked at Heaven Killer, then turned his head. His gaze fixed forward again.

“What about the cultists?”

“They just followed that foolish guy’s orders, but they are not without sin. I ordered them to enter the Demon Valley for a month as punishment.”

The old man said nothing. Heavenly Demon glanced at his back and spoke bluntly.

“I also think that rookie was wrong...”

“...”

“But I agree with one thing he said. Our lives are short. If the Heavenly Demon does not reincarnate before we die...”

“The Heavenly Demon will return.”

“I suppose so. Yes, as you say. But if He cannot find Himself before we die, isn’t that the same as not reincarnating?”

“What are you trying to say?”

The old man shot a sharp look at Heavenly Demon, who then smiled wryly.

“Until now, I thought waiting proved my faith to the Cult. But... I have other thoughts now. Maybe blindly waiting here is complacent.”

“I’ve said it repeatedly...”

“The situation in the Central Plains is unfolding interestingly.”

The old man’s eyes narrowed slightly as he stopped speaking.

Heaven Killer no longer mocked him but spoke with a more serious expression than the old man.

“There is a high chance of a great war, a war that will sweep across the entire Central Plains.”

“...”

“Do you understand? Even if we don’t intervene, the Central Plains will be engulfed in chaos. You know what that means, right?”

The old man’s mouth closed tightly, but unlike before, his lips twitched faintly.

“To Him, even that is a small matter. But not to us.”

Heaven Killer clenched and unclenched his fist slightly.

“Still, is just waiting really enough?”

“Your mortal desires have stirred.”

The old man’s words interrupted the conversation, and Heavenly Demon’s expression hardened.

“You said ‘child of Mount Hua’?”

“Why bring that story right now...”

“It makes me even more curious. After all, he’s just someone who hasn’t lived a hundred years. What kind of person could shake you so much?”

“...”

“Well, even if it’s just a label, the name Sword Saint can’t be thrown around to just anyone.”

The place that hates the Sword Saint the most is the cult.

If they could inflict even a small wound on Sword Saint’s soul instead of falling into endless hell, the cultists would do it without hesitation. Sword Saint is the one who dared to defile and destroy the absolute divinity that must never be harmed.

But conversely, the cult is also the place that most acknowledges Sword Saint.

The moment they belittle Sword Saint, the divinity of the Heavenly Demon who died by the Sword Saint’s hand also collapses. Therefore, to them, Sword Saint must exist as an absolute evil that must never be defiled.

But setting aside doctrinal issues, anyone who experienced Sword Saint during the war would never belittle him. They might curse, swear, and wail, but not belittle that being.

“Listen carefully, great high priest.”

“...”

“I’m not doing this because I want to fight. Nor because I’m tired of this place.”

“...”

“I am simply worried. If countless people are swept up in that war, even the great one who has not yet awakened cannot be guaranteed safety anymore. Some measures must be taken...”

“You just lack faith in Him.”

“Are you doubting my faith now?”

A sudden killing intent flared in Heavenly Demon’s eyes.

But the old man calmly absorbed the intent, like an unshakable tranquil lake.

Heaven Killer’s expression twisted slightly.

“I just don’t like that the measure of faith has become how long one can endure and wait.”

“That’s an excuse.”

“Oh, really?”

Heaven Killer’s eyes traced a strange curve.

“Indeed... I’m curious too. Whether you say just believe and wait because you truly believe He will find us.”

His mouth slowly twisted as well.

“Or... maybe you yourself are not sure He has reincarnated and are just afraid.”

“How dare you...”

At that moment, an immense demonic qi burst from the old man. His white robes quickly darkened to black, and a malevolent aura swirled around him like a hungry demon.

“Did I hit a sore spot?”

“Heaven Killer!”

“So don’t think of stopping me. I just think differently from you. If you think differently from me, I’ll find my own way.”

“He will...”

“At the last moments!”

Heaven Killer suddenly cut off the old man’s words with a howl.

“I don’t want to die regretting that I didn’t seek Him out at my last breath. I’d rather accept a glorious death at the hands of His reincarnated self as punishment for moving without His permission.”

“...”

“If you can stop me, try. But the only way to stop me is to kill me, great high priest.”

Heaven Killer turned away.

“That wouldn’t be so bad either, old friend.”

Heaven Killer left the hall without hesitation.

Left alone, the old man stared at the door Heaven Killer had closed behind him and sighed deeply.

‘This is why we should never set foot in the Central Plains.’

That place shakes the hearts of men.

That’s why he couldn’t send other cultists and sent Heaven Killer himself, yet even he was shaken.

‘Heavenly Demon...’

The old man closed his eyes.

“How could the Perfect One understand our imperfect suffering? Please... please reincarnate soon. Please...”

Incantations flowed again from the old man’s lips, endlessly continuing, echoing like a song, like a sob.

“Young Lord, where are you going?”

“Hmm.”

Namgung Do-Wei hesitated for a moment, choosing his words carefully.

‘What should I call this? A senior meeting? An executive meeting? A clan leader meeting?’

He was on his way to Mount Hua’s Sword Saint calling all sect leaders together, but he couldn’t think of a proper way to explain it to others.

‘Come to think of it, it’s quite chaotic.’

Generally, alliances have very strict rank distinctions. While sect leaders are collectively called so because it’s hard to assign them specific ranks, elders and senior members who work within the alliance usually have designated positions.

But strangely, only the Heavenly Friends Alliance had no such positions. Chung Myung’s free-spirited nature and Hyun Jong’s refusal to enforce such structure contributed to all of this.

‘Is it because the sect leading the alliance is the Taoist sect?’

If Namgung Do-Wei’s father, Namgung Hwang, were the alliance leader, he would have started by systematizing the alliance, creating leadership positions for each task and assigning suitable people.

‘Anyway, the more I see it, the more unique this place is.’

“Young Lord?”

“Ah, um... yes. The Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is calling.”

“Ah, you’re going to the boss group meeting.”

“...What meeting?”

The person who answered calmly was elbowed by someone beside him, causing him to suddenly stop talking.

“Ah, it’s nothing. Please have a good trip.”

“...”

“Please go and come back safely.”

“Y-yes... alright.”

As the family members bowed and greeted, Namgung Do-Wei turned around as if pushed.

‘Boss group?’

Well, how should he put it...

‘No. Even so, we...’

It didn’t seem like a proper term for those handling the major affairs of an alliance...

‘Wait a minute.’

Namgung Do-Wei stopped his hesitant steps forward.

So... there are many people, but the exact ranks are vague, and everyone with ability just roughly takes care of things... usually, the group just mindlessly follows the direction roughly decided by the smartest leader...

“...It’s a band of mountain bandits.”

No wonder it felt both strange and familiar! This is a mountain bandit group, this is!

Of course, the retainers probably didn’t call it the boss group for this reason, but it means they had a similar feeling.

‘This, this can’t go on!’

Shaking with determination, Namgung Do-Wei lit a fire in his eyes and quickly moved toward Hyun Jong’s quarters where the meeting was held.

‘I must change this!’

He engraved this firm resolve in his heart.

“Is everyone here?”

Having gained enlightenment and regained his composure, Namgung Do-Wei surveyed the surroundings with cold eyes.

‘Come to think of it, the people gathered here were strange from the start.’

All things in the world require proper form. Without it, people confuse public and private matters. All evils in groups arise from this confusion.

But look at this gathering. Aren’t the people here too disorderly?

“Seems like almost everyone is here?”

“...”

That very person is the problem. Namgung Do-Wei, who always looked at Chung Myung and the Five Swords with affection, today looked sour.

‘Taoist Chung Myung is unavoidable, but honestly, aren’t the others definitely problematic too?’

Don’t misunderstand. Namgung Do-Wei doesn’t think this because he dislikes them. He believes the Five Swords have the right to voice opinions on alliance affairs. But the problem is that without any rank, some come and some don’t as they please.

So...

“Yes, Sahyung. It seems everyone is here.”

Even Tang Soso is here, Tang Soso! And she’s not even one of the Five Swords!

‘No, recently, is she called one of the Five Swords?’

There were signs before, but Chung Myung cannot be lumped together with the other Five Swords.

Of course, the Five Swords are now somewhat embarrassing to call future warriors. Only Hae Yeon and Namgung Do-Wei can somewhat compare their skills at that age.

Those once called the Five Dragons are now handled by Yoon Jong or Jo Gul without Baek Cheon needing to intervene. Honestly, Tang Soso might even handle them. So the Five Swords are indeed formidable.

But Mount Hua’s Sword Saint Chung Myung is not someone to be discussed at that level. To find someone who can match him one-on-one, you’d need the top warriors from each sect.

‘Honestly, that seems difficult too.’

Those who saw Chung Myung fight the high priest alongside Jang Ilso cannot deny that Chung Myung’s skill is greatly underestimated.

Perhaps only ten people in the world can face Chung Myung. How can such a person be lumped under the name Five Swords?

So people gradually excluded Chung Myung from the Five Swords. They called the rest the Four Swords and often went on missions together. Recently, Tang Soso, who struck a cooperative blow at Plum Blossom Island, was quietly included, though unofficially.

‘Fine. Let’s say Mount Hua is like that.’

“No, the Green Forest King hasn’t arrived yet.”

Then why is that man here? Namgung Do-Wei looked at Tang Pae with slightly twisted eyes.

This is a sect leader-level meeting! So why is Tang Pae, the young lord of the Tang family, sitting here as if it’s natural? Tang Gunak is already here!

“Oh dear! I’m a bit late.”

“Ahem. Greetings.”

Namgung Do-Wei’s cheek twitched.

His gaze then fixed on Iron General Beon Chung, who followed the Green Forest King. Until now, he admired his armor-like appearance, but once annoyed, everything about him became irritating.

‘That person isn’t even an elder in the sect!’

If you are bringing someone to train, that should be an elder or something, so why bring that person to the meeting? Clearly, he’s not needed for a meeting that requires brains!

“Why do you look at me like that?”

“...Nothing.”

Namgung Do-Wei turned his head away with a deep sigh. His gaze now fixed on the root of all evil.

“Are you all here?”

That person. That very person! The root of all evil!

Namgung Do-Wei stared with sharp eyes at Chung Myung, who spoke in a nonchalant tone.

Everything has a cause. All this happened because that person, with the absurd title of Mount Hua’s third-class disciple, leads meetings without any proper rank.

Tang Pae, the Five Swords, and even Beon Chung!

Even if you object to their presence, the sentence ‘The Mount Hua’s third-class disciples are here too, so why single me out?’ refutes all complaints.

‘Something is definitely wrong here.’

The alliance will face more challenges ahead. So it’s necessary to establish a firm system now, for the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

“The reason I called everyone today is to discuss future matters.”

Chung Myung looked around and continued.

“For now, the major problems seem resolved, so we need to talk about what to focus on next.”

“Hmm.”

Tang Gunak nodded.

“You think the disciples’ training issues are basically settled?”

“Yes.”

“...The day has finally come.”

Everyone sighed simultaneously. It felt like finally finding light after a long, endless cave.

“But what do you mean by discussion?”

“Just as it sounds.”

Chung Myung shrugged.

“So far, we’ve done what had to be done. It was urgent.”

“That’s right.”

Those who watched Chung Myung push things forward with suspicion now agree it was necessary.

Originally, the Green Forest, Namgung, Five Palace warriors, and Central Plains people should never come together, but recently they interact without formality.

‘Though the lack of formality is problematic.’

Anyway, the integration is fortunate. What if the alliance went to battle without this process?

‘Just imagining it is terrifying.’

Tang Gunak shook his head.

He already experienced the destructive power when different sects united at Plum Blossom Island. If the Tang family hadn’t supported Mount Hua properly or Mount Hua hadn’t protected Tang at the front, the battle would have been very different.

Not just for harmony here, but for the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s future, the rigorous training was necessary, even if it felt like hell.

“The urgent fires are out, so now we must think about what to do next.”

“Hmm, indeed.”

The reactions split into two groups.

The first group admired, thinking, ‘So the Heavenly Friends Alliance actually discusses and pushes things together.’ Mostly those from Ice Palace or Beast Palace, who haven’t experienced Mount Hua much.

The second group was...

“Why have meetings? You’ll just do whatever you want anyway!”

People like the Five Swords distrusted the meetings Chung Myung held.

“When did I?”

“When? When? When did you say?”

“Tsk tsk tsk. That’s why you’re short-sighted! I’ve gathered everyone’s opinions without bias through meetings.”

“Sasuk.”

“Huh?”

“Is the prejudice I know different from the prejudice that guy talks about?”

“You’re stating the obvious stuff again. The only thing Chung Myung says that means the same as us is ‘I’ll smash that guy’s head.’”

“Ah, now I understand things a bit.”

“But really, these guys?”

When Chung Myung got angry, Hyun Jong reached out, grabbed his collar, and subdued him.

“I share the same thought.”

Those indifferent to Chung Myung’s words immediately sobered when Hyun Jong spoke.

“Speak, sect leader!”

“No! Look at the different attitudes! Are you discriminating between people now?”

“...Isn’t that necessary?”

“Huh? Uh... right. Yeah, we should discriminate a bit.”

No matter what, I shouldn’t be treated the same as the sect leader. Absolutely not.

Everyone sighed watching Chung Myung nod in sudden understanding. When will that guy ever mature?

Hyun Jong smiled gently and said.

“You’ve all worked hard so far.”

“No, alliance leader!”

“Everyone rushed here so much that the Heavenly Friends Alliance couldn’t properly do its duties. Let’s take this chance to bring up issues we’ve thought about and discuss what parts of the alliance need fixing.”

“As for things to fix...”

“I think just one person needs fixing?”

“But that Evil Sect bastard?”

“I didn’t say who. Why, are you speaking out?”

“Come out!”

As the quarters descended into chaos, Namgung Do-Wei boldly raised his hand. Hyun Jong nodded.

“Speak, Young Lord.”

“First!”

Namgung Do-Wei spoke vigorously.

“I believe we need to organize the chaotic Heavenly Friends Alliance system and establish proper ranks!”

He looked around at those present and declared.

“Even to fix this disorderly situation as it is.”

Some who felt guilty quietly averted their eyes.

“Position?”

Chung Myung’s reaction was rather indifferent.

“Is that really necessary?”

He looked around at those seated in the room and said.

“Well, even without that, everyone roughly knows who plays what role. There’s no real need...”

Then Namgung Do-Wei snapped and said.

“No, isn’t that a discussion just among us?”

“...Huh?”

“Just imagine if a war breaks out! The Four Sects Alliance invade. Then what will the small and medium sects along the Yangtze River do?”

“Well...”

“Won’t they all line up with us to fight alongside the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

Chung Myung looked at Namgung Do-Wei with a slight expression of admiration and said.

“Wouldn’t those people go to the Nine Great Sects?”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei was momentarily speechless and stared blankly at Chung Myung. The Five Swords nodded their heads and added a word each.

“If it were me, I’d side with the Nine Great Sects too.”

“Honestly, that’s overconfidence. To survive, you have to side with the Nine Great Sects.”

“Shaolin versus Mount Hua. Nine Great Sects versus Heavenly Friends Alliance. Eh, there’s no need to think about it.”

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyebrows twitched.

“Where does that confidence come from?”

“Sahyung, isn’t that guy from the Namgung Family? People from the Namgung Family usually think they’re no worse than Shaolin.”

“...Really?”

“If not would they act like that otherwise?”

Those bastards? Namgung Do-Wei barely suppressed his rising anger. In debates, the first to lose their temper loses. At times like this, he had to do his best to appear rational.

“Well... that might be true, but depending on the situation, wouldn’t there also be some who can’t join the Nine Great Sects and fight alongside us?”

“...I suppose so?”

Chung Myung reluctantly agreed with a reluctant voice. His will clearly showed, ‘I don’t know what nonsense this is, but since I said I’d listen whether it’s nonsense or not, I’ll endure it for now.’

“Think about it. Can those people know who’s who and properly follow orders?”

“Hmm?”

Those listening nodded as if acknowledging the point.

“Makes sense.”

“Hmm. That’s a problem I hadn’t thought of.”

“But... isn’t it just a matter of roughly following what others do? People have some sense, don’t they?”

Hearing that, Namgung Do-Wei slightly opened his mouth and looked at Jo Gul. Jo Gul asked bluntly.

“What’s that look? Like you’re saying ‘I didn’t expect the word ‘sense’ to come out of your mouth’?”

“Wow, you’re like a ghost.”

“Huh?”

“Ah, nothing.”

Namgung Do-Wei coughed loudly and continued.

“Of course, if people have sense, that might work. But efficiency drops. And think about it: an elder of a famous sect with white hair gives orders, then suddenly a very young kid jumps out shouting that it’s wrong and that they should go this way.”

“Uh...”

“Then whose orders should be followed? It’s hard to decide...”

“The young kid.”

“The young brat.”

“The very young and rude brat.”

“The one who’s the most annoyed among them.”

Namgung Do-Wei blinked at the almost simultaneous answers.

“Why, why?”

He was genuinely confused and asked again. Then Baek Cheon smiled contentedly.

“Is there anyone else in the Heavenly Friends Alliance who ignores the elders, gets annoyed, and only says what he wants?”

Wow, that’s really convincing.

Namgung Do-Wei was convinced. Then he quickly regained his composure.

“Th-that’s true, but that’s only known among us! Outsiders know the title of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint but don’t know what Taoist Chung Myung’s looks like.”

“Excuse me, Young lord.”

“Yes?”

“...We never said it was Chung Myung.”

“...”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei looked back at Chung Myung with trembling eyes.

Chung Myung smiled faintly as if saying, ‘Oh? So that’s how you saw it?’ Namgung Do-Wei instinctively avoided his gaze.

“Ahem, ahem.”

At times like this, it’s best to quickly change the subject.

“Anyway... to prevent confusion among those who don’t know that person well, a system and positions are necessary.”

At that moment, Yu Yiseol raised her hand.

Everyone was startled and looked at her. Even Tang Gunak couldn’t hide his surprise. Yu Yiseol had attended countless meetings before but this was the first time she tried to voice her opinion.

“W-well... okay. Speak, Yiseol.”

“Do they really not know?”

“...Huh?”

“Him?”

Yu Yiseol turned her head and looked at Chung Myung.

Yu Yiseol’s words were only two short sentences, but they convinced everyone.

“...Honestly, can they not know?”

“If you still don’t know, you’re not a martial artist.”

“Maybe they don’t know his face, but if they see that young brat rampaging in Mount Hua, even a passing kid would say, ‘Ah, that’s Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.’”

“If you don’t know Chung Myung, shouldn’t you have lived with your ears plugged?”

Namgung Do-Wei closed his mouth.

Thinking about it, that wasn’t wrong. The notoriety—or rather, the fame—of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was very high. Though it feels like an old faded story now, at one time, when he was called the Mount Hua Divine Dragon, he was recognized as the best upcoming martial artist in the world...

“Since the Hangzhou Demon Fire incident, he’s become even more famous beyond comparison.”

The opponent is none other than the Demonic Cult.

In reality, he fought alongside Jang Ilso against the high priest, but even the Heavenly Friends Alliance couldn’t reveal that truth plainly, so they just said Chung Myung and other members defeated the high priest.

The Four Sects Alliance claim that Jang Ilso captured the high priest.

Anyway, even the Nine Great Sects dare not openly dispute this fact, so the news that Chung Myung defeated the high priest has spread throughout the world.

Perhaps the name Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, Chung Myung is even more famous than the Heavenly Friends Alliance itself?

You can tell how famous a warrior is by walking through small alleys. If kids swing sticks shouting ‘Plum Blossom Sword Technique’ and ‘Mount Hua’s Sword Saint,’ then it’s all over.

“Th-that...”

Namgung Do-Wei was at a loss for words and hesitated, causing people to click their tongues.

“Young lord you tend to obsess over unnecessary things...”

Im So-Byeong did not miss this opportunity.

“Children from established families are always eager to not avoid unnecessary formalities. Since you’re the young lord of the Namgung Family, how much more so? If the Namgung Family became the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, even the janitor would have a position, like ‘Estate Environment Management leader’ or something.”

“Ugh...”

Getting hit by Im So-Byeong feels three times worse than from others for any human. He wanted to argue but since the example was poorly chosen from the start, he had no excuse.

But then, kind-hearted Hyun Jong saved Namgung Do-Wei.

“Hmm. It’s not something to be responded to like that.”

Everyone looked at Hyun Jong.

“Not everyone in the alliance is as famous as that Chung Myung guy, right?”

“That’s right, Alliance Leader.”

“Actually, positions create authority, so I don’t really like it either...”

Hyun Jong looked at everyone with a slightly embarrassed face.

As a Taoist who follows the teaching to let everything flow naturally, he wasn’t fond of limiting and subdividing people’s roles.

“Still, I agree that where people gather, appropriate positions are necessary. Problems can arise when elders of different sects have conflicting opinions.”

“Indeed...”

“It’s a sensitive issue.”

Tang Gunak and Meng So too nodded in understanding.

There isn’t much quarrel among sect leaders because some tacit rules have been established. But elders of each sect often can’t move beyond their own perspectives.

If there’s time to coordinate opinions, it’s not a big problem. But in war, where the situation changes moment to moment, small conflicts can cause big problems.

Especially...

“Most of the damage won’t be to us but to the small and medium sects fighting alongside us. We shouldn’t overlook this just because it doesn’t affect us much.”

“Hmm.”

“In that sense, Young lord of Namgung family made a very good point.”

“Alliance Leader...”

Tears of gratitude streamed from Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes as he looked at Hyun Jong.

Indeed, such deep thought and warm consideration, a character perfect for the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance!

Earlier, Im So-Byeong talked about the Namgung Family becoming the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, but that would never happen.

Originally, Namgung Hwang was one of the most arrogant people in the world. Even his son Namgung Do-Wei thought so. But even Namgung Hwang would have had to give up the leader position after meeting Hyun Jong.

It was truly fortunate that Mount Hua led the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Having such a person as leader...

“...But.”

At that moment, Chung Myung spoke with a slightly suspicious voice.

“Huh?”

“No, well... I’m not opposing, but I suddenly have a fundamental question.”

Tang Gunak asked with curiosity.
