“Well. It’s not like I don’t want the big bald guy to get mad and end up like a well-cooked octopus head.”

“More than anyone else, you would want that.”

“I think this bastard will ascend that day.”

“...Will the celestial realm accept him?”

“Ah... I hadn’t thought of that.”

If he actually ascends, the celestial realm would have to hold an emergency meeting. Accepting such a person as an immortal could lead to unpredictable consequences.

“Of course. Moreover, this isn’t just about feelings with Shaolin, is it?”

Tang Gunak continued in a serious tone.

“To screw over Shaolin... ahem! No, resolving old grudges with Shaolin is merely a side matter. The important thing is to strengthen the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s power and prove to the world that the Heavenly Friends Alliance is now equal to the Nine Great Sects.”

“Hmm.”

“As you know, people don’t accept new things easily. It will take more than a hundred years to gain greater influence than the Nine Great Sects by orthodox means.”

Even Hyun Jong nodded as if agreeing with this statement.

When Mount Hua was on the path to destruction, Hyun Jong’s dream was to someday rejoin the Nine Great Sects, even though he knew it was impossible.

If even Hyun Jong, who suffered the humiliation of being expelled from the Nine Great Sects, felt that way, how much more so others?

“But if one sect belonging to the Nine Great Sects can be made to choose the Heavenly Friends Alliance instead, that hundred-year task can be accomplished at once. Isn’t that so?”

“Well. That would be the case.”

But Chung Myung’s reaction was rather indifferent.

“Then why do you oppose? Isn’t it always your claim that the Heavenly Friends Alliance should be strengthened to oppose the Four Sects Alliance and the Demonic cult?”

“Ah. Sure, sure. Of course, it would be good if it could happen.”

“Hmm?”

Chung Myung grinned.

“Now, let’s decide. The Four Sects Alliance bastards who have gone crazy swarm Southern land. That lunatic Jang Ilso, with his eyes wide open, ‘Oh ho ho! You’ve crawled in well!’ will run at full speed to welcome us, breaking through the land like a bell.”

When Chung Myung snapped his fingers, Hyun Young spoke as if waiting for this moment.

“For reference, from river mouth, it takes 1200KM just to reach Zhanjiang, adjacent to Southern Island.”

“Eek!”

“1200KM?”

“And that’s in a straight line.”

Wide-eyed, Yoon Jong looked at Tang Soso.

“Soso, how far was it when we went to the North Sea?”

“Back then... um, it was probably about 2200KM since we started from Shaanxi.”

“Only 2200KM to the North Sea?”

Jo Gul’s voice came out hollow.

“We almost died back then too.”

“Exactly. I really thought we were going to die.”

“And there weren’t even enemies there. But now we have to go 1200KM through Southern lands, full of enemies?”

Everyone stared at the map in despair. Looking at the map of the entire Central Plains, they momentarily lost their sense of reality. Only after hearing ‘1200KM’ did it sink in.

“S-So, how long will it take?”

“If we consider normal travel speed... let’s see... if we can somehow cover 30-40Km per day...”

“Forty days?”

“If lucky, a month. And that’s only if traveling on well-paved roads.”

“Ha... haha...”

Baek Cheon burst out laughing.

‘This won’t work.’

To be honest, anyone who can survive a month in Four Sects Alliance-controlled Southern Land wouldn’t need to go all the way to the Southern Island.

That person would be a Martial God; why bother strengthening forces? They could just wipe out the Four Sects Alliance in that time.

“Well, let’s say we do go. Let’s say we reach the Southern Island.”

Chung Myung scratched his ear with a sullen face and said.

“Then what?

“What do you mean? Ah! I get it. Well, since the situation is bad, won’t the Southern Island sect cooperate?”

“No, what if they do cooperate?”

“...Huh?”

Chung Myung’s face twisted sharply.

“Let’s say they’re crazy enough to say, ‘Ah! We were already pissed off at that big bald guy!’ and decide to join the Heavenly Friends Alliance!”

“Yeah.”

“Then they just say, ‘Oh! Please take care of us!’ and leave? What’s different from now?”

“...The affiliation of Southern Island would change.”

“Ah, yes. That’s right. A very good thing. Then when the Four Sects Alliance bastards start beating the Southern Island Sect, they’ll just hear that we were watching from afar. That big bald guy will click his tongue and say, ‘If it were the Nine Great Sects, they would have helped. Damn it!’”

Just hearing that twists their stomach.

“...Then help... no, wait.”

Even Jo Gul cut off his own words mid-sentence. It was that absurd. How could they send troops over 1200KM away to help?

If they could send main forces to the southernmost lands, there’d be no need to talk about help. They would have already wiped out the Four Sects Alliance on the way.

“Hey. That’s thinking too one-sidedly.”

“Huh?”

Then Jo Gul chuckled and said.

“I’ve learned something from you too. At times like this, you think the opposite! If the Four Sects Alliance attack Southern Island, we invade the Yangtze! Then the front splits into two!”

“Oh, really? Then the big bald guy will happily clap beside the full-scale war between the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Four Sects Alliance! Good! Good! Both fight and both die!”

Jo Gul’s cheek twitched slightly. Chung Myung scolded.

“Hey, you crazy bastard! Do you think eating one sect as Southern Island Sect will give us the power to beat the Four Sects Alliance? Do you know what the tactic used by the weaker side is called?”

“W-Well?”

“It’s divide and conquer, you bastard! Divide and conquer!”

As the enraged Chung Myung tried to rush at Jo Gul, Hyun Jong quickly grabbed his collar.

“Chung Myung.”

“No, that guy is so infuriating...!”

“Yeah, yeah. But we’re in a meeting, so hit him later.”

“Ugh.”

As Chung Myung groaned, Jo Gul bowed deeply.

“Hmm. But Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, I think Jo Gul’s words have merit. The tactic of diversion isn’t only for the side with more troops. If we find a way to move the Nine Great Sects somehow...”

“Then Jang Ilso will stop it.”

“...Why?”

“You can’t think of that bastard as an ordinary person. He came all the way here to kneel if necessary to beat the Demonic Cult appearing on his land.”

“...”

“You plan to betray Jang Ilso?”

Chung Myung scoffed.

“I guarantee that the moment we push into the Yangtze, he’ll calmly pull his troops back.”

“...You mean abandon the Yangtze?”

“Tsk tsk. This is why the elders... no, the higher-ups...”

Chung Myung shook his head.

“They are trapped by the prejudice that once land is held, it can’t be easily given up, so they think Jang Ilso will resist. But Jang Ilso isn’t a defender; he’s an attacker. If we attack, he’ll gladly withdraw his troops. Then what should we do?”

“Well...”

Tang Gunak sighed.

“We have to keep attacking. Otherwise, it’s meaningless.”

“Yes. We have to attack all the way to Southern Island. How many kilometers?”

“...1200KM.”

“Yes, yes. Leading troops and dealing with those who stall, it would take about a month. In that time, Jang Ilso could finish off the Southern Island, build a villa in the South Sea, and play a few games.”

“...”

“What if Jang Ilso decides to return without that?”

Chung Myung stared coldly at the map.

“What we feared during the Demonic Cult incident will really happen. We’ll be trapped fighting in the middle of the Southern lands.”

“...Just thinking about it is horrifying.”

Distance is the problem, distance. Realistically, when the Southern Island sect is attacked, there’s no way to help.

Then Baek Cheon tilted his head in confusion.

“No, so... I don’t really understand why we’re here. We initially discussed not leaving the Hainan faction there. That Hainan faction...”

“Dong-Ryong.”

“Yes?”

Chung Myung looked at him with the most contemptuous eyes, and Baek Cheon flinched, looking around nervously.

“Ah, right. If we bring the Southern Island guys here, it’s over, right?”

“Y-Yeah. The ships are a problem, but somehow we’ll plan it well...”

“Yeah. Okay. They board ships and arrive on land. Then what?”

“What do you mean? Well...”

“Yes. The Southern Island has over five hundred people, and we have to break through the Southern lands with them.”

“...”

“And if we lose strength in the process, it’s meaningless, so we have to protect them intact.”

“Wow... that’s an incredible feat.”

“What, you bastard?”

“Ah, speak properly! With words!”

Hyun Jong pulled Chung Myung back, but this time Chung Myung couldn’t calm down easily.

“If I could do that, would I be worrying here? I’d just go down to the southern lands and chop off Jang Ilso’s head! Hey, you crazy fools! We’re strengthening our forces to beat the Four Sects Alliance, but you’re bringing up something that requires beating the Four Sects Alliance first? And everyone’s all excited about it? Hey, you guys! You’re making me sick to my stomach!”

Everyone cleared their throats and avoided Chung Myung’s gaze.

After grumbling for a while, he finally calmed down and exhaled deeply.

“The phrase chicken ribs (less meat more bones) is used for times like this. It’s good to have, but you lose more than you gain to get it.”

“...”

“It’s not that the big bald guy is careless and lets it be. The map is hopeless. If you meddle, it’ll only get worse.”

“Hmm, indeed...”

“We thought too much.”

“It’s a shame. It was a good idea.”

Those gathered nodded, understanding the practical difficulties. The plan wasn’t bad, but it wasn’t something to be handled lightly. It was better to leave it be.

Everyone accepted this fact. But one person, Yu Yiseol, silently stared at Chung Myung with a clear gaze amidst it all.

Pierced by that indifferent gaze, Chung Myung narrowed his eyes.

“What sago?”

“Then what about the Southern Island?”

“Huh?”

“...The people of the Southern Island?”

“...”

“Are they going to be abandoned like this?”

Chung Myung suddenly closed his mouth. His eyes darkened.

“Abandoned, you say, samae?”

Baek Cheon frowned and looked back at Yu Yiseol.

The Five Swords are comrades who can speak freely with one another. Having crossed the brink of death together several times, their bond might be even stronger than blood ties.

But regardless of their relationship, Yu Yiseol’s current words clearly crossed a line.

“This isn’t a situation we created, is it?”

“Yes.”

Yet Yu Yiseol only replied briefly in an unemotional voice.

“But the result is the same.”

“...”

“I was just asking.”

Having said that much, Yu Yiseol fell silent. Perhaps this topic could have ended here—if those gathered here were skilled in diplomacy and adept at hiding their thoughts.

But whether regrettable or fortunate, the conversation did not end there.

“...What will happen then?”

If Yu Yiseol was the one who asked the inevitable question, it was Yoon Jong who continued the topic.

Yoon Jong’s gaze turned to Im So-Byeong, the one who understood the situation best—or perhaps the one who could speak most objectively about it.

“Then what will happen to the Southern Island?”

“Well...”

Im So-Byeong scratched his head with the tip of his fan, looking troubled.

“Of course, the Southern Island Sect people are not fools; they know they are likely to suffer harm first. Naturally, they must have plans to escape—if they aren’t idiots.”

“Ah, naturally...”

At that moment, Im So-Byeong shrugged and added.

“But if it were something they could do alone, they would have already withdrawn from the Southern island.”

Yoon Jong’s expression, which had briefly softened with relief, stiffened again.

“They might still be observing the situation...”

“In a situation like this?”

When Yoon Jong could not answer, Im So-Byeong spoke indifferently.

“This situation may seem sudden to you all, but for the Southern Island, it has been a reality pressing against their skin for a long time. They probably knew for years that they might be forced into this. If I were the sect leader of Southern Island, when the Plum Blossom Island incident occurred at the Yangtze River, I would have chosen one of two options: either escape the island without looking back or strike at the Four Sects Alliance.”

“...That sounds right to do.”

“The fact they chose neither means there was a reason. I don’t know what that reason is. How could we know what is happening three thousand li away? But... I do have a suspicion.”

“If you have a suspicion...?”

“There are those from the Evil Sect who did not join the Yangtze war until the very end.”

“Lower District?”

“Yes.”

Tap.

Im So-Byeong lightly tapped his palm with his fan.

“Due to its nature of handling information, Lower District sect is a sect well-suited to monitor a wide area. Anyone with sense would have entrusted Hao Moon with watching the Southern Island Sect.”

“Wait a moment. The Lower District sect leader is in Hangzhou...”

“Is a sect leader necessary for surveillance? The underlings handle the work.”

“...”

“Perhaps the Lower District used the excuse of monitoring the Southern Island to avoid Jang Ilso’s pressure until now. But now, unable to avoid it any longer, they have completely submitted to Jang Ilso.”

Yoon Jong nodded as if acknowledging the logic.

“That means, wherever the Southern Island sect people land, Jang Ilso has already prepared to capture and kill them. That cunning man wouldn’t leave a knife at his back unused.”

Jo Gul tilted his head in confusion.

“If that’s the case, why hasn’t he eliminated them earlier? Why leave them be until now...?”

“Attacking a sect belonging to the Nine Great Sects is tantamount to declaring war on all of them. That would not be a choice Jang Ilso could make—at least not before the Plum Blossom Island incident.”

Jo Gul shut his mouth tightly at that, but Yoon Jong asked again.

“I may have digressed a bit... so what exactly will happen to the Southern Island faction?”

“I don’t know why you insist on asking me that, Taoist Yoon Jong.”

Im So-Byeong chuckled.

“Are you pretending not to know while clearly aware? Trying to act kind out of frustration in a helpless situation?”

Other Five Swords bristled at the sarcastic remark, but Yoon Jong calmly responded to Im So-Byeong.

“I just wanted to confirm the opinion of the Forest King.”

His unwavering eyes stared at Im So-Byeong.

“My thoughts are incomplete and brief. So I want to hear from someone who understands the situation much better than I do. Only then can I make the right choice.”

Im So-Byeong stared silently at Yoon Jong, then suddenly looked embarrassed and scratched his head again.

“Sorry. I spoke nonsense.”

Nodding, Im So-Byeong spoke candidly.

“Jang Ilso is not someone who leaves loose ends. Nor is he the type to spout nonsense about subjugating all Justice factions under him. What’s terrifying about Jang Ilso is that, though he is an absurdly ambitious dreamer, the way he realizes his dreams is brutally realistic.”

“Then...”

“The choice such a Jang Ilso would make is obvious, isn’t it?”

Im So-Byeong’s gaze darkened chillingly.

“No one will survive.”

“...”

“He will relentlessly hunt them down. Probably, anyone bearing the Southern Island name, child or adult, will be wiped out.”

A silence as quiet as a mouse filled the room. After a long moment, Yoon Jong was the first to speak.

“...Is there really a reason to go that far?”

“Well, of course there is a reason.”

Im So-Byeong answered nonchalantly.

“So that when the Four Sects Alliance enters the northern lands, riffraff cannot resist.”

“Riffraff?”

“Small sects, wanderers, hermits—those who, when the Evil Sect moves from the Yangtze into the Central Plains, can disrupt the main forces from behind and target supply lines.”

Im So-Byeong clicked his tongue.

“From the Four Sects Alliance’s perspective, these are the ones who have repeatedly thwarted their conquest of the Central Plains.”

“Ah...”

“They are setting an example for such people. Those hostile to the Four Sects Alliance will not have a single ant survive. Then, the small and medium sects who would have naturally joined the Nine Great Sects or the Heavenly Friends Alliance to fight together will hesitate, and even the warriors risking their lives will pause once.”

Yoon Jong’s face twisted.

“Just for such a reason... just to set such an example, they will use such cruel measures?”

“Just such a reason?”

But Im So-Byeong glared at Yoon Jong with an icy gaze uncharacteristic of him.

“Do you think what we are doing now is some kind of child’s play with sticks?”

“...”

“This is war.”

It’s not a shocking statement, but it was the first time those present truly grasped the gravity of the situation.

“Do not think of negotiation or agreement in war, taoist. In war, victory is the law, and the victor dictates terms.”

“Even so...”

“Well, that’s true.”

“Chung Myung!”

“More precisely, ‘No one remembers the agreements of the defeated.’”

Yoon Jong bit his lip silently. Chung Myung looked at him briefly, then spoke to Im So-Byeong.

“But that’s not all, right?”

Chung Myung’s clear gaze met his, and Im So-Byeong sighed softly and scratched his head.

“...Yes. In the past, that would have been all.”

As Yoon Jong and Im So-Byeong each took a step back, the atmosphere calmed.

“Ahem.”

And in that quiet atmosphere, Tang Gunak spoke.

“...the Southern Island is unfortunate, but I think it’s not something we can do anything about.”

A long sigh escaped his lips.

“The Southern Island sect was never part of the Heavenly Friends Alliance to begin with. Unless they reach out to us first, it might be presumptuous for us to offer help first.”

“Yes. It is presumptuous.”

The one who answered was none other than Baek Cheon.

“But Family lord, if no one does that presumptuous act, the Southern island sect will truly collapse.”

“Listen, Taoist Baek Cheon...”

“And.”

Baek Cheon spoke in a low voice.

“There were times when I desperately wished someone would do that presumptuous act at least once.”

“...”

“I couldn’t openly ask for help, nor could I lower my pride, but more desperately than anyone else.”

“That is...”

“If we turn our backs on the Southern Island here, both the Nine Great Sects and the Heavenly Friends Alliance will be abandoning them. They won’t be able to receive help from anywhere.”

Namgung Do-Wei, who had been silent until now, also spoke.

“I share the same thought.”

Feeling Tang Gunak’s gaze, Namgung Do-Wei smiled warmly.

“Thanks to that reckless help a few did, I am alive here now. I understand better than anyone the feelings of one surrounded and awaiting death.”

“Young Lord...”

“If someone is needed to go there, please tell me. Namgung family will not hesitate.”

“Listen, Young Lord. You must revive the Namgung Family...”

“I do not believe the Namgung path is to ignore what must be done now for the sake of many things in the future.”

“...”

“In that case, dying for a righteous cause would be a path more fitting for Namgung.”

Tang Gunak involuntarily looked at Hyun Jong, who only closed his eyes with a stern expression.

As Tang Gunak, frustrated, tried to speak, a soft laugh whispered in his ear.

Turning his head, he saw Chung Myung wearing a mysterious smile.

“A sect abandoned by all the Justice factions... Yes. That’s definitely a story I’ve heard many times somewhere.”

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Chung Myung’s cold gaze pierced both Baek Cheon and Namgung Do-Wei simultaneously.

“But listen carefully. Don’t think the price of that failure can be paid with your deaths. That failure could lead to the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s defeat and the Four Sects Alliance’s victory. Then your recklessness will drag everyone here into hell.”

“...”

“Do you think the others are silent because they lack unity? Grow up, you damn brats.”

Baek Cheon bit his lip and glared at Chung Myung.

“Then what exactly do you want to do? Just ignore it?”

“I...”

Just as Chung Myung was about to speak.

“A-alliance leader!”

Urgent voices poured in from outside the door.

“What is it?”

Hyun Jong, sensing the seriousness in the voice, quickly shouted. Then everyone in the room heard words that made them doubt their ears.

“The Shaolin Abbot is coming!”

“...What?”

Hyun Jong’s eyes widened to their fullest extent.

“Abbot, this is...”

Even as he walked toward the firmly closed main gate of the manor, Beop Jeong showed not the slightest hesitation.

Jong Ri-Hyung hurriedly followed with quick steps, his face troubled and unable to resolve his complicated thoughts in his mind, urgently trying to stop the abbot.

“Abbot. I was the one who said that killing time like this is meaningless. But even so, this is too much...”

“Amitabha.”

Beop Jeong softly uttered a Buddhist prayer and turned to Jong Ri-Hyung with a gentle smile.

“Sect leader.”

“Yes?”

“Thank you, Sect leader. Thanks to your words, my eyes have been opened.”

“...W-what do you mean by that?”

“You told me to think about the best possible outcome.”

“Yes, that’s right. I definitely said that. But...”

Beop Jeong slowly nodded.

“Thanks to those words, I now know what I must do, what the right path is.”

“B-but, abbot.”

“Right... yes, that is what ‘right’ means. I don’t know what I was seeing all this time. They say a small delusion grows endlessly and swallows a person—that’s called ‘Heart Demon,’ and it seems I was afflicted by it. Thanks to that, I was awakened as if doused with cold water.”

Jong Ri-Hyung wiped the sweat flowing down his forehead.

“Abbot... how could I possibly guess your deep intentions? But no matter how deep they are, suddenly visiting the place where they reside...”

“What could possibly be wrong with that?”

“If it’s that urgent, why not just summon them? I’m afraid people might misunderstand if word spreads that the Shaolin sect leader visited the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“Haha. That won’t happen.”

“Yes?”

Beop Jeong smiled gently and shook his head.

“Previously, Hyun Jong came to see me, so this time it makes sense that I come to see them.”

“H-how can that be logical? Our positions and statuses are different.”

“Well, I have no particular reservations. Rather, I feel fortunate to be staying not far from them. As soon as I made up my mind, I could meet them like this.”

“...”

“Don’t worry. Everything will be fine.”

A deep sigh escaped from Jong Ri-Hyung’s lips.

“May I ask what exactly you intend to do?”

“What could be so difficult to answer that? I intend to extend my hand to them.”

“What? A-abbot. Haven’t we already crossed an irreversible river with the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

“Not yet. It can still be reversed.”

Jong Ri-Hyung looked at Beop Jeong with a bewildered expression.

“T-then what if they refuse your proposal?”

“That won’t happen.”

“Even if...”

“Hmm, well, I don’t know. The ones I know wouldn’t refuse, but if such a thing happens...”

Beop Jeong’s eyes darkened slightly.

“No, that won’t happen. You don’t need to worry.”

Jong Ri-Hyung shook his head and followed behind Beop Jeong.

‘What on earth is he planning to propose in there?’

A man who had been agonizing inside the room for days suddenly burst out, heading straight to the Heavenly Friends Alliance without looking back. Strangely, Beop Jeong’s expression was unusually fresh compared to before.

‘Did he suddenly have a great awakening?’

It’s frustrating because even if asked, he won’t answer.

‘We can only watch and see.’

The guards at the main gate were flustered and unsure what to do when they saw Beop Jeong approaching. It was understandable; had they ever imagined the Shaolin sect leader visiting suddenly?

It was fortunate, if anything, that they had seen him before and recognized Beop Jeong from afar.

“Sect leader... no, is the alliance leader inside?”

“W-welcome, abbot.”

“Haha. No need to be so nervous. I just came to share some tea.”

“I-I’ve sent word ahead. Please, come in!”

“Did an order come to let me in?”

“N-no, not exactly.”

“Then I shall wait here. It is not proper for a guest to enter without the host’s permission.”

“Well...”

The guards exchanged nervous glances, unsure how to respond.

But then, fortunately, the inner door opened on its own.

Bang!

Hyun Jong, clearly flustered, forcefully opened the door. Seeing Beop Jeong and Jong Ri-Hyung standing there, he took a short breath and said:

“...Welcome, abbot.”

“Alliance leader, have you been well?”

Beop Jeong slowly bowed in greeting, and Hyun Jong returned the deep bow.

“If you had sent word earlier, I would have come to greet you at the gate, but how could you come suddenly without notice...”

“I just thought of it and stopped by. Once my mind was made up, I had no time to prepare formalities. Sorry, alliance leader.”

“Not at all. On the contrary, it is a great pleasure to have you here.”

“The person accompanying me is the Kongtong sect leader. You are acquainted, right?”

“I am Hyun Jong of Mount Hua. It is an immense pleasure to meet the Kongtong sect leader.”

“No, alliance leader. The honor is mine.”

Hyun Jong smiled brightly, but his eyes held a slight suspicion as he observed Beop Jeong.

The last time they met, Beop Jeong had shown raw emotions. But now, he seemed quite different—full of the ease and gentleness felt when they first met.

“Please come inside.”

“Then I shall take the liberty.”

Beop Jeong followed Hyun Jong into the manor.

Beop Jeong, a step behind Hyun Jong, soon came out into the courtyard and faced those waiting.

“It’s been a long time since I’ve seen you all.”

Beop Jeong greeted them with a warm smile. Some frowned slightly in displeasure, others nodded, but most tried to hide their reluctant feelings.

“Lord Tang is also here.”

“Isn’t this where I belong? It’s been a while, abbot.”

“Young lord as well.”

“...Glad to see you.”

Perhaps because their last meeting was unpleasant, Namgung Do-Wei did not show his usual neat courtesy.

“And these?”

“Yes. These are the lords of the Beast Palace and Ice Palace.”

“Ah.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently.

“I am truly glad to meet such distinguished guests.”

“I am Meng So.”

“I am Seol So-Baek.”

This time, Beop Jeong did not bow deeply but gave a light nod.

Beop Jeong slowly raised his head and his gaze fell on a person behind. As if waiting, a voice burst out.

“What are you sneaking in here again to scavenge... uh! Uh uh! Ugh!”

Baek Cheon, Yoon Jong, and Jo Gul simultaneously covered Chung Myung’s mouth.

For the first time, a sigh escaped from Beop Jeong’s lips.

“You haven’t changed.”

“Ugh! Ugh!”

Chung Myung, his face flushed red, shouted something while Yu Yiseol and Tang Soso held his arms and legs, but unfortunately, it was inaudible.

Beop Jeong shook his head and turned to the others, speaking in a gentle tone.

“I would love to chat with you all, but since I am not here for personal matters, please understand I cannot stay long.”

“Ah, no.”

“You must have urgent matters to attend.”

“Thank you all for understanding.”

Beop Jeong’s gaze reached Hyun Jong.

“Alliance leader, I would like to speak with you privately for a moment.”

“Very well, abbot.”

Beop Jeong’s gaze then fell on Tang Gunak.

“And Lord Tang as well.”

“I will do so.”

Just as the others, except Hyun Jong and Tang Gunak, were about to step back.

“Young lord will also come.”

“...You mean it?”

“Yes.”

Namgung Do-Wei nodded with a stern face.

“Understood.”

Beop Jeong bowed toward Meng So and Seol So-Baek.

“Originally, I would have invited both of you, but since what I am about to discuss is a sensitive matter within the Central Plains, I cannot recommend your presence in there. Please forgive me.”

“Do not worry. We fully understand.”

“Yes, it’s fine.”

Beop Jeong smiled faintly and looked at Chung Myung, who was still struggling and making muffled sounds, with a peculiar expression.

“You come in as well, and the other Mount Hua disciples beside you too.”

“...Us too?”

“If you weren’t here, who would stop Mount Hua’s Sword Saint from beating me to death when the time comes?”

“W-what do you mean...”

“Hahaha!”

Jo Gul’s face turned red at Beop Jeong’s joke.

“By the way... it seems one important person is missing?”

“Yes?”

At that moment, Yu Yiseol gestured toward Beon Chung.

“There.”

“Huh?”

Yoon Jong slightly peeked toward where Yu Yiseol pointed and frowned.

“...What on earth are you doing there, Green Forest King?”

Im So-Byeong, crouching behind Beon Chung’s broad back with his head covered, startled and quickly darted his eyes.

“H-ha ha ha... I...”

“Hmm. So you are the Green Forest King.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently as he looked at Im So-Byeong.

“How about it? Will you come?”

“Eek!”

“Huh?”

“I-I was wrong! Sorry! I won’t do bad things again!”

“...”

“I-I didn’t mean to become a bandit...!”

“That Green Forest...”

“Aaaah!”

Im So-Byeong screamed in a high-pitched voice and ran off behind the pavilion.

Everyone stared blankly at the scene. Even Beop Jeong blinked with his mouth open. Yoon Jong, dumbfounded, asked Baek Cheon in disbelief:

“Why... why is he like that?”

“Hmm.”

Baek Cheon scratched his cheek awkwardly.

“I heard from that guy before that if you gathered all the bandit corpses beaten to a bloody pulp by Shaolin monks, it would fill half the Yangtze River.”

“...”

“They dislike Namgung, but fear Shaolin the most. It’s said it’s etched in blood, and no matter what you try, it won’t work.”

“Does that make sense?”

“They say only those scared stiff of the big boss survive in the Green Forest, but I don’t really understand what that means either.”

“Then why is it different with Monk Hae Yeon?”

“They say apostate monks becoming bandits is standard, so they’re not afraid.”

“...He’s not an apostate.”

Beop Jeong shook his head in dismay at Im So-Byeong’s absence. Hyun Jong awkwardly guided him.

“Please come this way.”

“Yes. Thank you, alliance leader.”

As those who were to attend together headed inside, Seol So-Baek, who remained in the courtyard watching, tilted his head.

“He seems a bit different from what I heard.”

“In what way?”

At Meng So’s words, Seol So-Baek slightly frowned.

“From what I heard, he seemed like a really bad person...”

“But seeing him like this, he seems better than expected?”

“...Yes. Maybe that’s just how he looks?”

“No. Perhaps that monk Beop Jeong really is a good person as you think.”

“...Yes? Then why...?”

Meng So slowly crossed his arms and, looking at the door Beop Jeong entered, murmured softly.

“Sometimes, a righteous sage can be more terrifying than a villain.”

Seol So-Baek tilted his head as if finding it hard to understand.

The atmosphere in the room was very strange.

Of course, to those present, Beop Jeong was not a stranger. Hadn’t Beop Jeong visited Mount Hua several times before to initiate talks?

However, usually the ones who spoke with Beop Jeong were either Hyun Jong or Chung Myung. This was the first time he sat with so many people. So the atmosphere was inevitably delicate. No one dared to speak first.

Naturally, it was Hyun Jong who broke the silence.

“I should have served some tea, but I was unprepared.”

“Not at all, Alliance Leader.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently.

“It seems this is the Alliance Leader’s residence, and it is quite modest. It reflects your character perfectly.”

“It’s just that living away from home, I haven’t properly furnished it. It’s not modesty.”

“Haha. You remain humble. I feel ashamed whenever I see you, Alliance Leader.”

“Put some saliva on your big mouth... gulp! cough!”

Those who naturally covered Chung Myung’s mouth smiled awkwardly, while Yu Yiseol and Tang Soso poked his ribs from both sides.

“......”

When Beop Jeong looked at Chung Myung with an expression saying “You’re something else,” Hyun Jong, his face flushed red, apologized on his behalf.

“I’m sorry... As you know...”

“There’s no need to apologize.”

Beop Jeong chuckled softly.

“The eccentricities of ordinary people are rude, but those of heroes become legends, don’t they? Mount Hua’s Sword Saint already has that qualification.”

Hyun Jong sighed deeply. A truly proud disciple, but sometimes, really, truly embarrassing.

“I have nothing to say...”

“It’s not just words.”

“Yes?”

Beop Jeong smiled gently and said.

“Considering what Mount Hua’s Sword Saint has accomplished in Hangzhou and for the world, I should be the one showing respect before him. Isn’t it obvious who has helped the people more?”

“......”

“So please don’t mind.”

“Thank you for saying that.”

Hyun Jong answered reluctantly, his mind becoming a bit busy.

‘What is he thinking?’

Of course, he couldn’t fully grasp Beop Jeong’s true intentions, but there was no pretense in his words or attitude.

Therefore, Hyun Jong didn’t know how to respond. His feelings were already hurt, but he couldn’t just be disrespectful.

“Abbot.”

“Please speak, Family Lord.”

Tang Gunak looked sternly at Beop Jeong and spoke.

“It’s good to exchange kind words, but why have you come here?”

“Hmm!”

The one who cleared his throat unpleasantly was none other than Jong Ri-Hyung.

“They say the Tang family lord is the second-in-command of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, and his power is indeed great. Seeing someone who isn’t even the Alliance Leader urging the abbot is telling.”

Tang Gunak glared silently at Jong Ri-Hyung, who met his gaze without backing down.

“That’s right. I was just too eager with joy.”

But their brief confrontation was naturally eased by Beop Jeong’s gentle voice.

Beop Jeong slowly bowed deeply in respect.

“First, on behalf of the Nine Great Sects, I express gratitude to those who subdued the calamity in Hangzhou.”

“How...”

“It was a partnership that will be remembered. My insight was limited; I did not expect you to accomplish this so excellently.”

“......”

“And please forgive me for my narrow-mindedness in not coming sooner to express thanks.”

“What are you saying, Abbot? We are just grateful you came here now.”

As Beop Jeong continued to humble himself, Hyun Jong’s posture lowered even more.

Seeing this, everyone present deeply felt the power of the Shaolin Abbot’s position. Despite all the insults and gossip, just having the Abbot sitting before them made them nervous, especially with such respect shown.

“If you’re just going to thank us after it’s over, you should have helped from the start.”

“Chung Myung!”

“Did I say something wrong?”

He seemed calmer now, so they let him go, but Chung Myung snapped. Baek Cheon quickly tried to cover his mouth again, but Beop Jeong nodded obediently.

“You’re right. I was foolish.”

“......”

“And the one who should receive this thanks more than anyone is you. Thank you.”

Chung Myung, who had been staring blankly at the bowing Beop Jeong, suddenly glared fiercely.

“Ah! I got it!”

“Uh, huh?”

“You bastard, you’re the leader of the Lower District sect!”

“...Huh?”

“Where do you think you are, daring Evil Sect bastard acting like the bald... gulp! cough!”

Chung Myung was dragged away again. This time Yu Yiseol, Tang Soso, and even Hae Yeon joined in poking his ribs.

“Ughhh!”

But those trying to stop Chung Myung were equally bewildered. Especially Jo Gul and Tang Soso looked at Beop Jeong with faces that clearly thought, ‘Did this guy eat something wrong on the way here?’

“Is it strange?”

Understanding their feelings, Beop Jeong asked Hyun Jong with a smiling face.

“Rather than strange...”

Hyun Jong covered his mouth and coughed softly before continuing.

“You seem a bit different from before, so I’m a little taken aback.”

“You need not think so. I just stepped back a little and observed from behind.”

“From behind?”

“Amitabha.”

Beop Jeong quietly nodded.

“Though I knew that the greatest danger for a martial arts practitioner is their ego, I had unknowingly been trapped by mine. I have only just realized it.”

“......”

“Everyone pursues their own good. Forgetting that a different good is not wrong, I was forcing only my own good.”

“...Abbot.”

“If what I thought was wrong turned out to be right, I should have admitted my mistake... Yes, Alliance Leader. But I could not accept it. So I shouted that what was not wrong was wrong. The more I did, the more my delusion grew.”

Hyun Jong shivered. What Beop Jeong said resonated with Mount Hua’s way.

Beop Jeong smiled softly.

“So I let go. Isn’t it just emptiness around us?”

“Yes, Abbot. Yes.”

Hyun Jong nodded in agreement.

“Then today...”

“I came to apologize and to make one more proposal.”

“A proposal?”

“Yes, a proposal.”

Beop Jeong said with a smile.

“After letting go of everything, I realized what I am doing now, and that because of my ego, everyone is going down the wrong path.”

Hyun Jong hesitated and asked.

“Do you mean that all of us are on the wrong path?”

“Yes, that’s right.”

“How...”

“...Amitabha.”

Beop Jeong closed his eyes, softly chanted, then slowly opened them. Meeting Hyun Jong’s gaze steadily, the seriousness within made Hyun Jong tense.

“I believe Alliance Leader knows better than anyone the current situation in the Central Plains.”

Seeing Hyun Jong nod, Beop Jeong continued.

“The south of the Yangtze suffers from the Four Sects Alliances’ villains, who will soon invade north of the river. And after a hundred years, the Demonic Cult has reappeared. The phrases ‘like a candle in the wind’ and ‘on the edge of a cliff’ are not exaggerations.”

“Hmm, that’s right.”

“But what about us who must protect the Central Plains? We only keep clashing with each other.”

As Chung Myung’s eyes sharpened again, Beop Jeong spoke with slight self-mockery.

“I know that most of the fault lies with me. That’s why I came to apologize. I’m truly sorry. I am too lacking...”

“Ah, no, abbot. How could it be only your fault? Mine is great as well.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently.

“Hearing that from you, Alliance Leader, I feel even more ashamed. Having lived in a well, I thought the world would flow as I wished it should. Knowing otherwise, I should have reflected and accepted it, but I couldn’t let go of my tiny pride and twisted my view of the world.”

“Abbot...”

Hyun Jong looked at Beop Jeong with trembling eyes.

If the Shaolin abbot humbles himself this much, shouldn’t his sincerity be trusted? Even Jong Ri-Hyung sitting beside Beop Jeong couldn’t hide his surprise at such words.

“Then, your proposal is...”

“Originally, when the Four Sects Alliance flourished and the Demonic Cult rose, what should we have done? The answer was obvious. We just failed to recognize each other and took a long detour.”

“......”

“Therefore, I propose to you, Alliance Leader, that it’s not too late. Before irreversible events occur, why not unite now?”

Hyun Jong nodded without hesitation.

In truth, this was what Hyun Jong had long wished for. He just couldn’t trust Beop Jeong and the Nine Great Sects, but if they humbled themselves and wanted to cooperate, why would he refuse?

“If the abbot says so, naturally the relations...”

“No, Alliance Leader.”

“Yes?”

But Beop Jeong shook his head firmly.

“That is not enough. Do you know why I could not accept nor embrace Mount Hua?”

“Well, I don’t know.”

“Because it was not mine.”

The ambiguous words made Mount Hua disciples frown, but Beop Jeong clarified.

“To be precise, it was because we were not under the same fence. When I realized what I thought was united was not, I felt resentment and anger. Yes, this is all my fault. But, Alliance Leader, the world is not only for the capable. Some are as lacking as I am.”

“...That may be so.”

“To such people, the unity of the Nine Great Sects, the Five Great Powers, and the Heavenly Friends Alliance feels like mere formality. They pretend to be friendly now but will eventually split again.”

Everyone listening nodded. Even they themselves would find it hard to escape that perception.

“Then what do you propose?”

“We must abandon it all. Let go of what we hold. And first, we must tear down the fence. There is only one way to do that.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently, uttering words like thunder, bewildering, yet perhaps long hoped for.

“Disband the Heavenly Friends Alliance and return to your original places. Shaolin formally requests Mount Hua’s return to the Nine Great Sects.”

A gasp of shock escaped from the mouths of those present, instantly filling the room.

“Nine... Great Sects... One Union...”

“...Return?”

A groan-like voice escaped from the mouths of Mount Hua disciples.

Their eyes constantly watched the abbot, as if to confirm whether what they just heard was true. The shock was that great.

But the shock they received was nothing compared to what Hyun Jong and the elders felt.

Kkuuuuk.

Hyun Sang’s hand gripping his thigh tightened as if it would pierce the flesh at any moment. But Hyun Sang could not even feel the pain.

Return to the Nine Great Sects. Who could have imagined such words would come from the abbot?

The disciples do not know. The Baek disciples and Chung disciples here cannot even imagine what this word means to the Hyun disciples.

They watched with their own eyes. They remembered clearly the deaths of Mount Hua’s predecessors who were expelled from the Nine Great Sects, who could not bear their resentment until the moment they closed their eyes, hoping that someday in the distant future, Mount Hua’s name would rise again in the Nine Great Sects.

Their master also repeatedly urged them before leaving. He pleaded until the end to restore Mount Hua to the rock and regain its glorious past, weeping and begging, even though he knew it was a dream that could never be fulfilled.

But now, someone with the power to achieve that has spoken of returning.

Of course, the abbot had mentioned the same words in the past. But back then, ‘Mount Hua’s return to the Nine Great Sects’ was nothing more than a sweet bait and an empty promise to manipulate Mount Hua at will.

But now Mount Hua is no longer a place the abbot can manipulate at will, nor a place where empty promises can be thrown and easily appeased. Mount Hua is now a sect that can hold the abbot accountable for its words.

The fact that the abbot, who cannot be unaware of this, brought up these words means this proposal is sincere.

“Th-those words...”

Hyun Jong, who was about to ask if it was sincere, suddenly closed his mouth. Whatever he says now would feel awkward. He is both the sect leader of Mount Hua and the Alliance leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

The condition for disbanding the Heavenly Friends Alliance must not be a fruit that returns to Mount Hua.

“That is an excessive statement.”

At that moment, Tang Gunak spoke.

“Mount Hua is the core of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. No matter how great the Nine Great Sects are, nothing is greater than the headship of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“Hmm. One could think that way.”

The abbot also readily acknowledged that fact.

“And what do you say about the remaining sects if the Heavenly Friends Alliance is disbanded?”

“Would that be a problem?”

“...Yes?”

The abbot smiled faintly.

“The awkwardness is that the Tang family belongs to the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Is it really a problem for the Tang family to return to the Five Great Families where it originally belonged? The Tang family has been there for hundreds of years.”

“...”

“The same goes for Namgung. What is so strange about the Namgung Family using the name of the Five Great Families again?”

“We have no such intention.”

Namgung Do-Wei answered coldly, and the abbot shook his head.

“Young lord, I have nothing to say to you.”

“...”

“But if young lord truly cares for the family, you must let go of your stubbornness a little.”

“I believe that being with the Heavenly Friends Alliance is for the good of the family.”

“That may be true now. But what about a hundred years from now?”

“Of course...”

“Do you think it will benefit the family to belong to the Heavenly Friends Alliance after the leader is gone, the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is gone, and everyone here has returned to dust?”

“...”

“It is not so. No. I am not unaware of the lofty ideals and noble aspirations of my Heavenly Friends Alliance. But that...”

The abbot declared calmly.

“It was not created by the Heavenly Friends Alliance, but by a few people here. Am I wrong?”

Namgung Do-Wei’s face twisted.

“It is a matter of carrying on that will.”

“It is not easy to carry on the will of predecessors in a single sect, so do you really think it is possible for the Heavenly Friends Alliance, a coalition of many sects, to carry on that will across generations?”

“...”

“Young lord, you are a fine person. That may be so. But young lord, there is no guarantee that the next generation of young lord will be like you. And the generation after that even less so.”

The abbot shook his head.

“The Heavenly Friends Alliance will not last long.”

“No, but...”

“It is not because the Heavenly Friends Alliance is lacking, but because it is too much. A person’s capacity has limits from the start. Can they truly bear the broad will of the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

No one could easily answer that.

“I know because I have experienced it. I was not someone who thought my capacity was small, but I did not understand the will of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Then I ask, what happens if someone like me appears among the descendants of the Heavenly Friends Alliance? If I appear in an era without the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint and without elder Hyun Jong, what will happen to the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

“Uh...”

This time, no one dared to speak. The answer was obvious, but saying it aloud would be a slight against the abbot.

“Even so, is maintaining the Heavenly Friends Alliance truly for the future?”

At that moment, Baek Cheon spoke sharply and coldly.

“I do not know if I may speak, abbot, but the Heavenly Friends Alliance includes not only us but also the Palace. You did not call them here to say this...”

“We will remove the label of Palace attached to the Northern Sea Ice Palace and the Nanman Beast Palace, and recognize these two sects as Central Plains sects.”

“...”

“And I promise to support them as much as now, if not more. Then they will obtain what they have long desired. What the Heavenly Friends Alliance cannot give: to enter the center of the Central Plains proudly, not as border barbarians.”

“W-what about the Green Forest! The Green Forest is part of the Evil Sect but also in the Heavenly Friends Alliance!”

At Jo Gul’s sudden shout, the abbot smiled faintly.

“That is also a concern of mine. It is something we can discuss together. But isn’t Green Forest already a place that cannot be called Evil Sect?”

“...That is true, but...”

“If the Green Forest promises not to harm commoners anymore, there is no reason not to recognize them as part of the Justice faction. The Buddha also taught to lead evildoers to repentance, so wouldn’t that be a good thing?”

“I-if that is so...”

Jo Gul scratched his head and stepped back.

If Im So-Byeong had heard this, he might have rolled his eyes and sworn loyalty to the abbot. No matter how much Mount Hua favors Green Forest, it could never have the impact of Shaolin’s single recognition.

“Then isn’t that enough?”

At that moment, Chung Myung, who had been quietly listening, spoke.

“I think I already gave an answer to that before. We have no reason to return to the Nine Great Sects, which is nothing but a facade...”

“Is it enough if it’s not just a facade?”

“Huh?”

The abbot looked intently at Chung Myung and smiled faintly.

“I promise to grant Mount Hua equal status with Shaolin.”

“What?”

Chung Myung inadvertently spoke informally for a moment. Even he, who thought he had the abbot’s tricks in the palm of his hand, was shocked by this statement.

What kind of place is Shaolin?

It is a place that never lowered its noble pride as the head of the Nine Great Sects, even when Mount Hua alone was fighting the Demonic Cult in the past war.

But now, compared to Mount Hua at that time, which was still far from enough, they say they will grant a status that even then was not obtained.

“What nonsense...”

“I will declare to the world in the name of the Shaolin Abbot that Mount Hua and Shaolin will have equal status and cooperate with each other.”

“...”

Chung Myung’s mouth dropped open.

“Um... Abbot. I... I’m just asking in case.”

“What is it?”

“Have you been wetting your pants lately, or forgetting where you are... Gyaek!”

Chung Myung collapsed forward on the spot. Hyun Young quietly withdrew the foot that kicked Chung Myung’s back of the head.

“Sorry, Abbot. This guy’s mind wanders away from time to time...”

“And the elder is?”

“My name is Hyun Young.”

“...I will remember that.”

The abbot blinked, as if surprised that among Mount Hua’s elders, there was someone who could stomp on Chung Myung.

“Amitabha.”

The abbot, as if calming his slightly startled heart, looked straight at Hyun Jong.

“Alliance Leader.”

“...Yes, Abbot.”

“Of course, I know this alone is not enough. This is too small a price to pay for what Mount Hua currently enjoys and the path it seeks to take.”

At that moment, Chung Myung jumped up and nodded vigorously. The abbot spoke calmly.

“But even so, Alliance leader, you must accept my proposal.”

“...What do you mean by that?”

“If you are only the sect leader of Mount Hua, you may refuse my proposal. But as the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, you must not refuse it.”

Seeing Hyun Jong’s hesitant expression, the abbot smiled faintly.

“The reason is simple. Because the Heavenly Friends Alliance is exactly the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Tell me, when the Nine Great Sects and the Heavenly Friends Alliance are in conflict like now, when they are divided and cooperate minimally, and when they lay everything down and become one again.”

Hyun Jong’s face hardened.

“Which of the three is easier to deal with them? And which of the three can save more people?”

“Well...”

Hyun Jong could not finish his answer, but that was an answer in itself. Even a child could answer that question on the spot.

“I believe that the Heavenly Friends Alliance has never lied about fighting for the common people. You have already proven that sincerity.”

The abbot scanned those seated here.

“So I dare to ask. Shaolin will let go first. So please, do not refuse the chance to save more people and save the world because of pride. I beg you.”

Everyone became so quiet it seemed they were holding their breath. The abbot slowly bowed his head there.

Tang Gunak, with a face hardened beyond measure, bit his lip tightly.

‘This is... a dead end.’

The justification the Heavenly Friends Alliance had upheld and the values it tried to protect were consensus. It was everything to the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

But now that consensus had become a sharp dagger aimed at their necks.

‘The abbot...’

Tang Gunak, suddenly feeling intense fear toward the abbot, unconsciously grabbed his own thigh tightly.

A pressure came over him as if someone was slowly, very slowly tightening a large rope around his neck.

‘So this was it.’

Jong Ri-Hyung twitched his lips and looked at the back of Beop Jeong, who had bowed his head.

‘No way...’

At first, he thought Beop Jeong had lost his mind.

How could the other sects of the Nine Great Sects accept the words of restoring Mount Hua and giving up everything they could?

This was nothing less than an unconditional surrender declaration to the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

But the moment Beop Jeong spoke his last words, Jong Ri-Hyung realized his true intention.

‘There is nothing to lose.’

What damage would come to the Nine Great Sects by recognizing Green Forest as part of the Justice faction? Even if those external sects were recognized as Central Plains sects, would they suddenly abandon their lands and flock here to settle?

Tang Gunak and Namgung are just returning to their places. So practically, Shaolin is only conceding by recognizing Mount Hua.

Of course, creating a counterpoint to Shaolin’s sole leadership of the Nine Great Sects by Mount Hua would not be pleasant for Shaolin. But...

‘It’s incomparable to having the Heavenly Friends Alliance outside.’

No. On reflection, putting Mount Hua as a counterpoint might not be bad for Shaolin. Unless Shaolin had an unshakable position, but currently Shaolin is clearly shaken, isn’t it?

What if a sect opposing Shaolin comes from outside in such a situation?

Other sects that don’t want to lose power to Mount Hua will surely rally under Shaolin. The reason Shaolin is being attacked now is partly Beop Jeong’s mistake, but also because Shaolin is perfectly above the other sects.

People always want to pull down the one who stands highest.

‘But people hate someone surpassing them even more than the one who stands above them from the start.’

No one wants two people standing above their head. If that happens, Shaolin will rather get a chance to recover its shaken position.

‘The more I think about it, the more perfect it is.’

If Mount Hua accepts this proposal, Beop Jeong will instantly become the hero who unites the divided Justice faction against the Four Sect Alliance and the Demonic Cults. A hero who sacrificed for the world by giving up what he had!

What’s more astonishing is that all this was originally what Beop Jeong should have done. If things had gone naturally, it would have happened like this, but it was achieved dramatically through many pains.

People usually care more about dramatic resolutions than natural victories, don’t they? The saying ‘turning misfortune into fortune’ exists for times like this.

‘What will you do, Hyun Jong?’

Jong Ri-Hyung’s gaze fixed on Hyun Jong, whose expression had hardened.

Of course, if the Heavenly Friends Alliance rejects this proposal, it will be all for nothing, but such a thing is unlikely. The moment they reject it, all the justification and values the Heavenly Friends Alliance has upheld will be completely destroyed.

And what remains will only be the judgment that they ignored the sacrifices of common people in pursuit of power.

But how on earth could they reject this proposal?

Jong Ri-Hyung could see it. The dagger of Beop Jeong lodged in Hyun Jong’s side. A dagger that pierced flesh, split bone, and tore into the lungs in an instant.

“Hmm...”

It was the moment Hyun Jong swallowed a hollow breath as if groaning in pain.

“Why... why must it be us? If so, then Shaolin should just join the Heavenly Friends Alliance! Wouldn’t the same result come if the Nine Great Sects and the Five Great Families joined the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

Jo Gul, sensing something was wrong despite not understanding fully, shouted hurriedly. His words were excessively rude for a third-class disciple of Mount Hua to say to the Shaolin sect leader.

But Beop Jeong did not blame Jo Gul; rather, he smiled as if he found it admirable.

“Of course it’s possible. But that cannot happen.”

“...Why?”

“Because the common people are also those who fight alongside us. Which name would they be more familiar with?”

“...That is...”

Beop Jeong sighed.

“And shamefully, the other sects of the Nine Great Sects would refuse to dissolve the Nine Great Sects and join the Heavenly Friends Alliance. That would only cause further division.”

“W-why only us...”

“So I ask this of you. I believe there is no better way than this.”

Beop Jeong looked at the remaining people.

“There is only one principle. To unite strength in a better direction to save more people. If you know a better way than mine, teach me. If it is reasonable, I will surely follow.”

“...”

There is no such method.

Even those here all accepted it. The method Beop Jeong spoke of was the strongest means to oppose the Four Sect Alliance and the Demonic Cults.

‘Fight alongside the Nine Great Sects?’

It made the struggles to strengthen forces earlier feel foolish. Of course, those efforts were not entirely in vain, but the name of the Nine Great Sects was that huge.

Tang Gunak bit his lip slightly and said.

“Earlier, you promised equal status to Mount Hua. But in the end, isn’t that just words? Is that realistically possible?”

“Why do you think it’s impossible?”

“Even if Shaolin recognizes us, other sects won’t.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently.

“Why worry about that? Then those here can help, can’t they?”

“...”

“Changing affiliation won’t erase the bonds you’ve built. Isn’t that more certain support than those flimsy promises and status?”

“The Nine Great Sects’ case is a bit...”

“Tang Gunak. There is nothing perfect in this world.”

Beop Jeong shook his head and said.

“If you don’t give up even one thing you have and refuse to concede even one point, harmony and compromise are just dreams.”

Beop Jeong slowly turned his gaze toward Hyun Jong.

“Isn’t that right, sect leader?”

Hyun Jong just laughed.

Until recently, they called him the Alliance leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance without fail, but now they call him sect leader as if he were already part of the Nine Great Sects. Quite a clever phrase.

“I have one question, sect leader.”

“Please ask.”

“Why are you going this far?”

Beop Jeong looked at Hyun Jong with unwavering eyes.

“You must be doubting my true intentions now, sect leader.”

“...Honestly, yes.”

“Then I will be honest. I am now very personal, blatant, and full of desire.”

“Yes?”

“One day in the future, I never want to hear that the crazy monk Beop Jeong rampaged without knowing what to do and turned the world into a den of the Evil Sect.”

Hyun Jong flinched.

“If my words sounded like a threat, it’s because I am that desperate. I may not become a hero who saved the world, but I do not want to be a traitor who plunged the world into misery. If that happens, I won’t have the face to meet Shaolin’s ancestors even after death. I’d rather fall into endless hell and suffer forever.”

Hyun Jong’s fingertips trembled slightly.

Beop Jeong was simply calmly revealing his inner thoughts, but to Hyun Jong, it sounded like a sharp warning. That if he rejected this proposal, he would become the traitor.

Even though he knew it was not the intention.

“Therefore, I intend to do what I originally should have done, even if late. Regret is late no matter how early, and the past cannot be undone, but isn’t it better than not regretting at all?”

“...That is true.”

Hyun Jong could no longer understand.

The one sitting before him seemed like a snake filled with deadly poison, and at the same time, like a high monk who had abandoned all worldly attachments and attained enlightenment.

No, perhaps both were one and the same.

‘I don’t know.’

He could not understand. Beop Jeong’s actions and goals were those of a monk, but the results of his acts were pushing Mount Hua into a corner. The supreme good was pressing him like a severe evil.

‘Perhaps... the abbot was like this from the beginning.’

When he believes something is right, he strives to enforce that right with all his might. In the process, he fears no sacrifice, endures loss of face, and stubbornly withstands momentary humiliation.

Yes, this is just the image Beop Jeong has shown from the opposite side until now. The difference is that now his goal has changed to be with them.

Can one say that is wrong?

‘What should I do?’

Everyone is looking at Hyun Jong.

Those who were passionately discussing the future of the Heavenly Friends Alliance just moments ago now looked at his mouth with faces that didn’t know what to do.

All these great people here were completely swayed by Beop Jeong.

No, without exception?

Hyun Jong’s gaze naturally turned to one person. The one who always opened the way whenever he was cornered.

“Mount Hua in the Nine Great Sects...”

Before Hyun Jong’s gaze could reach, Chung Myung’s relaxed voice flowed like water in the room. That voice naturally drew everyone’s attention.

“Well, it’s not bad. If the sect leader keeps all he said.”

“If there’s no trust, I’ll even swear an oath. The price can be my neck.”

“Well, if you say that much.”

Chung Myung shrugged his shoulders.

“Then will the Nine Great Sects become the Ten Great Sects? The name sounds a bit off.”

Beop Jeong chuckled.

“What’s so important about the name? The Nine Great Sects means ten sects, but it has long become a symbol itself. So even if eleven sects join, people will understand.”

“Well, that might be true.”

Chung Myung nodded with his arms crossed.

“Abbot.”

“What?”

“You’re more impressive than I thought. Honestly, I never imagined you’d be cornered this much.”

Beop Jeong smiled gently at those words.

“Hearing this from Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, it seems I did make a good proposal.”

“I’m so happy I want to dance. Since when did you think of this?”

“I just saw it after letting go.”

“...Maybe I should try Buddhism.”

Chung Myung shook his head as if he couldn’t stand it. Beop Jeong, watching him closely, spoke again.

“So, what will you do? Your answer?”

“...I can’t think of a way to refuse. Honestly, if we don’t accept this, Mount Hua will be cursed throughout the world until it bursts.”

“That’s simply because it’s the right thing. Isn’t it?”

“...That justification and path was the sword I wielded, but to think the abbot would wield it.”

Chung Myung clicked his tongue loudly and raised both hands slightly, as if surrendering.

“Then can I take it as acceptance?”

“Well, I suppose so. I don’t know about later, but for now, the priority is to respond positively and discuss.”

“Good. I don’t expect an immediate answer here either. Take your time to think.”

Beop Jeong’s smile showed his confidence. Chung Myung looked at him and said.

“But before that, I have one question.”

“What is it?”

“You said earlier it was to save even one more person? And that’s why you gave everything up?”

“That’s right.”

“Are you sincere about that?”

“Of course.”

“Oh, really?”

Chung Myung smiled slyly. Their eyes met with a playful look.

“Then what about the Southern Island Sect?”

“...Southern Island Sect?”

“Yes, the Southern Island Sect. Surrounded by the Four Sects Alliance at the southern tip of the Jiangnan region.”

“...”

“I’ll decide after hearing that. Tell me, what will you do with them?”

Those who had been holding their breath there clearly saw the piercing cold gaze of Chung Myung piercing through Beop Jeong.

A white hand revealed beneath a white silk robe lightly grasped a red wine cup.

Jang Ilso tilted his head back, slowly blinking as he gazed at the moon hanging in the sky. After silently staring at the moon for a long while, he shifted his gaze to the wine cup in his hand.

The cup was filled to the brim, as if it might overflow at any moment. The moon reflected inside it shimmered and flickered.

“When I sing, the moon wanders...”

A whisper-like voice slowly flowed from his lips.

“When I dance, the shadows scatter wildly.”

Jang Ilso brought the cup to his lips and slowly tilted it.

“When awake, we enjoy together; when drunk, no trace remains...”

The empty cup held neither wine nor moonlight anymore. Yet Jang Ilso silently just stared at the empty cup.

“That’s Li Bai.”

Ho Ga-Myung approached and spoke softly.

“I didn’t know Alliance Lord favored the Poet Immortal.”

“...Li Bai.”

Jang Ilso murmured with a smile.

“I don’t like him. No... I rather dislike him.”

“You mean Li Bai?”

Jang Ilso’s gaze shifted to the river. The moon flickering on the water’s surface was visible.

“Li Bai... drowned trying to scoop the moon reflected in the river.”

“...”

“How foolish.”

Jang Ilso threw the cup he held into the river in one swift motion. The ripples disturbed the moon’s reflection on the water.

But the trembling moon soon regained its form, calmly.

“That’s just an illusion. No matter how much you try, you can never catch it. But still...”

A faint smile blossomed on Jang Ilso’s lips.

“It doesn’t disappear. So you can’t help but watch it, as if enchanted.”

“But there’s the real moon in the sky, isn’t there?”

“You can’t reach it.”

Jang Ilso tilted his head back as if letting it hang.

“The moon in the sky can’t be reached no matter how desperately you stretch your hand. But the moon on the river seems catchable if you just reach out...”

“...”

“So Li Bai must have drowned too. He wanted to have that moon no matter what.”

After staring at the moon on the water for a long time with half-closed eyes, Jang Ilso’s gaze turned to Ho Ga-Myung.

“What do you think, Ga-Myung? Do I look like I’m trying to catch the moon reflected in the water?”

Ho Ga-Myung gave a small smile at those words.

“I don’t know about that, but I do know how to get the unreachable moon in your hand.”

“...How?”

Ho Ga-Myung silently walked over, placed a new cup before Jang Ilso, and filled it with wine. The moon rose again over the full cup.

“Here it is.”

“...”

“It disappears when you drink it, but if you refill it, you have it again, don’t you? Don’t regret the lost moon; just pour more wine.”

Jang Ilso chuckled softly.

“The truly clever one is someone else.”

Jang Ilso reached out and slowly drank the wine Ho Ga-Myung poured.

Ho Ga-Myung, silently watching, spoke with a slightly heavier voice.

“I failed to persuade Deadly Lone Tiger.”

Jang Ilso did not answer and only stared at the river.

“He said he would rather die than follow Alliance lord.”

Ho Ga-Myung spoke softly, seeing Jang Ilso still silent.

“...There was no other way, so I executed him.”

“I see.”

Jang Ilso slowly opened his mouth.

“Did he have family?”

“I believe he has a wife and son in the Fukien region. Though he himself left home at least ten years ago...”

“A heartless man.”

“Isn’t that how such people usually are?”

“Send enough money to the remaining family to last a lifetime.”

Ho Ga-Myung looked at Jang Ilso without answering.

“Why?”

“It might be a bit presumptuous, but... he was someone who rebelled against the Alliance Lord. Foolish enough to serve the wrong lord and ultimately make the wrong choice. Why bother with his family...”

“Wrong...”

Jang Ilso turned his head back to look at the river.

“Yes... foolish enough to enter the service of the wealthy lord, foolish enough to remain loyal to the end. Humans are not dogs, yet he lives like a dog, a fool of the world.”

“Indeed.”

“But... well. Is that really wrong?”

“...Alliance Lord?”

Jang Ilso chuckled softly.

“If the world could be clearly divided into right and wrong, what would be difficult? You’d just have to decide who is right.”

“...”

“But Ga-Myung... everyone has their own ‘rightness.’”

Ho Ga-Myung slowly nodded.

“That’s why it’s hard. No one is wrong, but there’s only one path to choose.”

“Then what should we do?”

“Someone will persuade, someone will try to suppress with logic. Someone will wait, someone will plead, and someone will be moved. But I just...”

Clang!

The cup in Jang Ilso’s hand cracked sharply and then shattered into pieces.

“You just trample and take it. That’s the fastest way.”

Jang Ilso lifted a half-full bottle of wine and slowly poured it. The spilling wine slowly flowed into the river.

“I don’t feel sorry for those I’ve trampled, but can’t I at least offer a cup of wine? Isn’t that right?”

Ho Ga-Myung quietly nodded.

“I will follow your orders.”

“Good. Let’s do that.”

“Yes, then.”

Ho Ga-Myung bowed and quietly withdrew, and Jang Ilso’s gaze returned to the moon on the river.

‘Who is right...’

It was hopelessly meaningless.

Beop Jeong’s lips quietly closed.

Chung Myung’s clear gaze pressed coldly upon him. It was like a sharply frozen blade piercing deep into his lungs.

Darkness had fallen, and in the lamp-lit room, only those eyes shone brightly.

This was not the first time he saw those eyes. He had seen them many times before.

And each time, Beop Jeong had to struggle to hide his true intentions, trying to avoid the inexplicable discomfort felt from the eyes of a child who had barely lived half his life.

Yes, discomfort...

Though he says it now, that discomfort might have been another form of fear. Fear felt from someone who should never instill fear, so even he himself did not understand it.

Beop Jeong quietly closed his eyes.

After honestly admitting his inner feelings, his mind calmed. When he came here, Beop Jeong had already expected such a moment.

‘Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.’

Someone he could never understand no matter how hard he tried. Someone who always went opposite to his path and achieved unimaginable feats.

He himself was a threat to Beop Jeong and to Shaolin.

Therefore, Beop Jeong had tried to control Mount Hua’s Sword Saint by any means. But after deep suffering, he realized why he feared Chung Myung.

‘Because he was not mine to have, because I could not imprison him within me, I was afraid.’

If Shaolin had someone like Chung Myung, would Beop Jeong have feared him? Would he have regarded him as a threat and tried to suppress him?

No, absolutely not. He might have welcomed Chung Myung’s existence more than anyone else. Someone who could fill what Shaolin currently lacked.

What does this mean?

‘If you can’t understand him, keep him close.’

Because the moment the other is no longer an enemy, fear will also disappear.

Therefore, Beop Jeong’s conclusion was one: to somehow embrace Shaolin and Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, preparing an offer they could never refuse.

Beop Jeong slowly opened his eyes.

“Taoist.”

Chung Myung faced Beop Jeong without a word, his gaze unchanged.

“To be honest... I anticipated this moment even before coming here.”

“...What do you mean?”

Beop Jeong smiled faintly.

“I didn’t know what questions you’d ask, nor how you’d react. But I thought you’d definitely bring up something I didn’t expect.”

Beop Jeong smiled again.

“You’ve always been like that.”

Chung Myung’s lips twitched slightly. He sensed that Beop Jeong was different from usual.

“So before coming here, I set one principle.”

“What is that?”

“No matter what questions I face.”

Beop Jeong stared at Chung Myung unwaveringly, not avoiding his gaze even an inch.

“I will never utter a lie.”

Chung Myung frowned.

“It’s natural for a Buddhist to do so, but it’s absurd for a Buddhist to say that. It means you’ve been lying as if eating rice until now.”

“Maybe so.”

“Shaolin seems finished too. The abbot calmly says such things.”

Chung Myung lightly reproached, and Beop Jeong shook his head.

“I never intended to lie. But sometimes even the truth becomes a lie.”

Chung Myung clicked his tongue shortly, understanding what Beop Jeong meant.

“Sounds like you won’t say obvious things like ‘I’ll try my best’ or ‘I’ll take responsibility.’”

“Amitabha.”

Beop Jeong softly chanted the Buddha’s name.

“That’s right. Sometimes in this position, you have to speak lies that aren’t lies. You don’t think they’re lies yourself because you try to keep your word. But... not telling everything is just another form of lying.”

Beop Jeong looked at Chung Myung and thought.

‘How ridiculous.’

How did it come to this?

Even to him, Chung Myung was a hero of the world. No one could deny that now.

If he truly made it his life’s goal as Shaolin’s abbot to protect the murim world and restore its spirit, he should have welcomed someone like Chung Myung gladly.

But... until recently, Chung Myung’s existence was nothing more than a confusion filling his heart.

“So I will speak honestly.”

He knows.

What he must say here is a well-packaged lie. It makes his position look better.

Saying he’ll do his best to save Southern Island isn’t hard. Asking Heavenly Friends Alliance for help isn’t difficult either.

But Beop Jeong knows it’s just another form of lie. Hasn’t he already decided not to utter even a little lie here?

“You asked if I truly will do my best to save even one more person.”

“...Yes.”

Beop Jeong nodded.

“Then I will speak honestly.”

Beop Jeong’s dimmed gaze met Chung Myung’s in the air.

“Shaolin... I have no intention to save the Southern Island Sect.”

His voice was so calm that it pierced deeply and clearly into the hearts of those seated there.

Chung Myung glares at Beop Jeong.

His eyes have not changed at all. Yet everyone felt that the meaning contained in Chung Myung’s gaze had changed.

“Do you even know what you’re saying right now?”

“I do.”

“Even so...”

“Listen to the end.”

“...”

Beop Jeong cut off Chung Myung’s words and continued speaking.

“Furthermore, I will not allow any sect aligned with Shaolin to head south of the river.”

“Abbot...”

“That goes without saying.”

Beop Jeong’s gaze swept over Chung Myung and Hyun Jong, who was seated behind him.

“The Heavenly Friends Alliance will also formally be requested to restrain from going south of the river, regardless of the outcome of this agreement.”

Chung Myung’s face became expressionless.

“If we refuse?”

“We will stop you.”

Beop Jeong answered calmly.

“I will do everything in my power to stop you.”

And he added words that perhaps did not need to be said.

“Even if the cost returns painfully to Shaolin.”

A heavy silence fell over the room.

Not a single breath was heard.

Not only because the meaning behind Beop Jeong’s words was so grave, but also because his expression and atmosphere were extremely solemn.

It did not fit.

The implication of Beop Jeong’s statement was too harsh. It was not something someone who pursues Buddhism could dare to utter.

However, contrary to the harsh meaning, Beop Jeong’s manner of speaking was so serious it felt almost noble.

These two contradictory aspects coexisted, giving those watching Beop Jeong an indescribably strange feeling.

“As I said.”

Chung Myung’s cold voice broke the strange atmosphere and spread.

“Do you know what you are saying right now?”

Beop Jeong calmly looked at Chung Myung, who was scolding him with a terrifyingly emotionless face.

“Of course I do.”

“No, I think you don’t.”

Chung Myung’s lips parted slightly, revealing his teeth. An uncontrollable passion seeped from him despite his efforts to restrain it.

“You said you would do your best to save even one more person.”

“That’s right.”

“Isn’t Southern Island made up of people?”

Chung Myung’s voice was so cold it felt deadly.

Hearing that voice, Beop Jeong silently closed his eyes without answering.

Perhaps displeased even by that attitude, Chung Myung’s voice grew more intense.

“I asked if those at the southernmost tip of the Central Plains, surrounded by the Evil Sect and enduring each day with a heart bleeding dry, are not people.”

“...”

“They are people too. And they are those who have pledged to the Abbot’s cause. They have been striving within the fence of the Nine Great Sects until now.”

Naturally, Chung Myung’s feelings toward the Southern Island Sect were not good.

From the start, Chung Myung could not have good feelings toward the sects belonging to the Nine Great Sects, and after all, the Southern Island was a sect that had swallowed the place originally belonging to Mount Hua.

Though he did not harbor particularly strong animosity toward the Southern Island, there was no reason to look upon them favorably.

Yet, the abbot’s attitude was enough to provoke Chung Myung’s anger.

“Do you think it’s a contradiction?”

“A contradiction?”

Chung Myung’s lips twisted into a sneer.

“It’s not a contradiction but sophistry. If not, then hypocrisy!”

“Amitabha.”

Beop Jeong loudly chanted the Buddhist invocation and slowly opened his eyes.

At that moment, a slight flinch appeared on Chung Myung’s face. He saw that Beop Jeong’s gaze, struck by sharp criticism, was no different than before.

Beop Jeong met Chung Myung’s gaze with a low, deeply settled look.

“It might seem that way. Or perhaps you are right.”

“...”

“Yes. You are right. Shaolin should save them. But I ask, is that the only right thing to do?”

Chung Myung’s face twisted slightly, as if he found it hard to understand what Beop Jeong was saying.

“Why?”

“Are you asking because you don’t know? That is...”

“Because it is a natural and just agreement.”

Beop Jeong spoke as if cutting off Chung Myung’s words.

Yet Chung Myung did not get angry but rather nodded. Beop Jeong’s words were exactly what he wanted to say.

Helping those in danger is so natural that it doesn’t even need the grand term “agreement.”

“Then I will ask one thing, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Beop Jeong quietly opened his mouth.

“There is a way to save a hundred people. Then, should the martial artists and Buddhists naturally do that?”

“That would be natural.”

“Even if a thousand lives are lost as the cost?”

“...”

Chung Myung closed his mouth.

“I ask again.”

But Beop Jeong did not stop speaking.

“There are only two paths to choose from. The path to save a hundred and the path to save a thousand. Which should be chosen?”

“What sophistry is this...”

“Is it really sophistry?”

Beop Jeong shook his head.

“The Southern Island is at the southernmost tip of the Four Sects Alliance. To help them, conflict with the Evil Sects Alliance is inevitable, which means the Nine Great Sects would be declaring war on the Evil Sect Alliance first. It will surely lead to full-scale war.”

“Are you saying you are afraid of that?”

“I am afraid.”

Beop Jeong nodded nonchalantly.

“So afraid that I shudder. Thinking about how many will die in that war keeps me from sleeping.”

“...”

“Aren’t you afraid?”

Chung Myung could not answer that.

Because in that feeling, they were no different.

“It is an unavoidable war.”

“I know. I know better than anyone.”

Beop Jeong also did not deny Chung Myung’s words.

“But Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, even if the war is unavoidable, there is a difference in how it is fought. If we enter enemy territory and carry out reckless plans, those who need not die will perish.”

“...”

“More people than those saved in Southern Island, perhaps several times more, may have to die. Yet... is carrying this out truly an agreement?”

“If it is not done without regard for one’s own benefit, it does not deserve to be called righteousness.”

Hearing that, Beop Jeong slowly shook his head.

“Listen, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

“...”

“I will not die.”

A low sigh escaped from Beop Jeong’s lips.

“No matter the operation, I cannot stand on the front lines.”

“Well...”

“Is it right for someone who will not die to push others into death under the name of righteousness?”

Chung Myung’s eyes flickered with rising emotion.

“It’s always like that.”

“...”

“I heard that excuse during Plum Blossom Island too. It’s the path for everyone. It was unavoidable. We had to endure to save more. So? What was the result?”

“...”

“Isn’t it just fear? Abbot!”

A hollow laugh escaped from Beop Jeong’s mouth.

“Did you say I was afraid?”

“Yes.”

“An absurd question. Isn’t it only natural?”

“...Yes?”

“Aren’t you afraid?”

“...”

Beop Jeong and Chung Myung’s eyes met in the air. But at that moment, both felt together.

Each other’s gaze, the helpless fear hidden deep within.

The fear only those who understand that people can die because of their choices can have.

“I have thought many times about Plum Blossom Island. Many times.”

“...”

“What exactly did I do wrong? What mistake did I make? Was my choice really wrong?”

Beop Jeong turned his gaze to Namgung Do-Wei.

Namgung Do-Wei glared at Beop Jeong with almost no emotion, but Beop Jeong remained calm.

“In the end, I reached that conclusion. Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, and Namgung’s Young Lord. Even if I went back to that time, my choice would not change. I would still watch Namgung’s death without intervening.”

“...Abbot!”

Namgung Do-Wei gritted his teeth.

But Beop Jeong only shook his head facing Namgung Do-Wei’s anger.

“Listen, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

“Speak. Go ahead.”

“Do you really think I should have led others to Plum Blossom Island?”

“Isn’t that obvious?”

“Then I ask, if I had led Shaolin and the alliance to attack Plum Blossom Island before you came, what do you think would have happened?”

“Well...”

Chung Myung closed his mouth.

They would have saved them. Shaolin is never weak.
